Absolute Pharmacy Workflow
Absolute Pharmacy Workflow
Overview
Absolute serves as a backup pharmacy for Semaglutide, Enclomiphene, and Testosterone Cypionate. These medications are primarily filled through the Pharmacy Hub or Curexa Pharmacy. Since Absoluteâ€™s processing costs are higher, it should only be used when our partner pharmacies are unable to supply the necessary medications.
â€‹
Absolute does not require signatures for delivery, unlike Curexa. They ship to all states we operate in except VA, AL, and NY.
Please note that we do not currently use Absolute Pharmacy for any patient orders. Any orders sent there were done in error.
Absolute Pharmacy Portal (Refills and Clarification Only)
The Absolute Rx Portal provides access to see orders. The dashboard is the landing page when accessing the portal.
Finding Orders
To locate order information, navigate to "Orders" in the left-side menu. Use the search bar to look up an order by order number, Rx number, or patient's name. When searching by name, you may filter by order status such as Filled, Shipped, Completed, or Cancelled. Selecting an Order # displays full order details, including tracking information.
Viewing Order Details
Clicking into an order provides the following information:
- Order status and stage
- Patient delivery details
- Payment method
- Shipping type
- Signature requirements
- Tracking number
- Photo of delivered package
Canceling Orders
If the order has not shipped, you can cancel it directly from the order details page by selecting 'Cancel Order' and confirming. Please note that you cannot cancel orders in the 'verification', or 'shipping', stage. The option to cancel would appear next to 'RX Hard Copy.'
Triggering API Calls
From the Intercom sidebar, open the Patient Chart app and select Retool. Under the Medications section, check the box next to the medication(s) to be shipped. If multiple options from the same pharmacy appear, the medication with the earliest effective date is preselectedâ€”uncheck any items that should not be included.
Next, scroll to Manual Medication Trigger:
1. Select the shipping method (FedEx 2Day).
2. Check the box labeled â€œCheck when readyâ€ to enable the trigger.
3. Click Trigger Medication Refill.
Once triggered, the API history will appear next to the Manual Medication Trigger section, and the order will populate in Akute and the Absolute Portal.
Handling Overnight Shipping Requests
Orders typically ship within 24 hours. If a patient wants overnight delivery, open the order details, select the existing shipping type, and change it to FedEx Priority Overnight. Save the update to finalize the change.
Work Addresses (TRT Only)
Absolute can ship non-controlled substancesâ€”including GLP-1 medications and Enclomipheneâ€”to nearly any address (home, Walgreens, workplace, etc.). Controlled substances (TRT), however, may only be shipped to verified work addresses. A direct signature is required, and only the patient may sign.
Contacting Absolute Pharmacy
Use the #fountain-absolute Slack channel for all issues related to orders from Absolute Pharmacy.

Platform Guides
Akute Workflow
Akute Workflow
Overview
AkuteHealth is an Electronic Medical Record (EMR) system. It offers solutions for providers and CS team to effectively manage patient information, medical notes/ forms/ documents, lab orders, and more. Additionally, it integrates with Retool for order management and Stripe for billing purposes.
AkuteHealth
Akute Main Menu
The left-side Main Menu in Akute is available for the following purposes:
Inbox - To find the list of all tasks (i.e. for lab order, refill script, etc.) and faxed lab results
Patient List - To search for a patient and manually add a patient if they are not signed up by the system automatically
Patient - To check a specific patientâ€™s details, preferences, progress notes, medications, labs and other documents
Patient Sidebar
When you pull up a patient's profile, you will have access to the following features for different purposes:
Tasks - To view or create tasks, such as lab orders or refill scripts.
Medications - To view the current medications of the patient.
Labs - To check lab results and quickly access the Healthgorilla page.
Notes - To review progress notes entered by the provider.
Documents - To view or manually add documents, such as lab results.
Demographics - To view and edit the patient's details, such as address, date of birth, phone number, and email address.
Forms - To view updates on patient's treatment.
Orders - To view Lab orders.
Faxes - To view or resend the documents that was previously sent to the patient.
Tasks
Akute Tasks - Quest Orders (TRT)
Click on Add Task
Enter the following details:
Task: Quest
Tags:
For New Signups:
â€˜new labsâ€™
â€˜Quest - provider must includeâ€™
For Monitoring Labs:
â€˜f/u labsâ€™
â€˜Quest - provider must include diagnosis codesâ€™
Priority: Urgent
Due Date: Current date
Task Owner: Labs
Click on Save
Akute Tasks - Labcorp Orders (TRT)
Click on Add Task
Enter the following details:
Task: LC [appointment date]
Tags: new labs
Priority: Urgent
Due Date: Current date
Task Owner: Labs
Click on Save
Akute Medications
If you click View Pharmacy, a pop-window will appear, showing where the patient's medication is from.
Akute Labs
Patient lab results can be found in this section. Click Order Labs to quickly access the Healthgorilla page if you need to locate a missing lab order that does not appear on Akute.
This will take you to the landing page where you can create a new order. To go to the homepage, simply click on the HealthGorilla logo (gorilla icon).
Akute Notes and Sharing Medical Notes
If we need to know who was the provider the patient last saw, when the last video visit was, or when the routine labs (next lab visit) are due, we can review the Progress Notes in this section.
These are documents created by healthcare professionals to update a patient's medical records.
Sharing Medical Notes
Click the kebab (:) menu below the patient's name on Akute
Select Print Clinical Profile from the options
When the Chart Export screen appears, click Generate PDF to automatically export all the charts
Download and add the file in the Akute Documents
Send a secure message called 'Medical Notes,' and attach the medical notes to the message.
Documents and Faxes
-If we need to check for Faxes on Akute we can click on 'documents', on the sidebar.
Akute Forms (GLP)
GLP patients submit weekly forms that provide updates on their treatment.
The patients will receive an automated text on a weekly basis/ The text will contain a link to the form and once they submit the form, it will be uploaded to their Akute chart.
Akute Orders
This shows the list of lab orders created for the patient.
Facility: Where the lab tests will be done
Orders: Tests ordered for the patient
Date: When the lab order is created
Reference #:Lab Order ID or Requisition Number (6-digit order ID is for Labcorp/ Quest)
Akute Faxes and Faxing Documents
Faxes section shows you the fax history and allows you to view or resend the document that was previously sent over to the patient.
Akute Tasks - Labcorp Orders (TRT)
Go to Documents
Click the document to be faxed over for the patient
Click the kebab (:) menu button at the right side of the file name
Select Fax from the options
Complete the Fax Details
Click Generate
Instructional Video
Faxing without a patient chart:
-On the off-chance we receive a fax not intended for a patient in our care or our practice, we can fax over the document. This is an uncommon occurence, but the process for dealing with this is below.
â€‹
Upload the document to Inbox->Documents
Click into the document
Click the 3-dot icon at the top right
Select the fax option
You can then select a recipient and add a cover letter message. There is no additional customization of the outgoing fax
Akute Sticky Notes
Akute Patient Portal - Inviting Patients
Akute Health has a Patient Portal that displays labs, vitals, and notes in viewable sections. When a patient requests to see their lab results, we need to check the Labs section to confirm that the lab results have been successfully added to their profile.
If lab results are available on Akute, follow these steps to invite a patient to access their lab results through the Patient Portal.
This option might be greyed out if they were previously invited to the portal.
Click on the kebab (â‹®) menu button below the patient's name
Select Invite to Patient Portal
Send the Akute: Sharing Lab Results macro
We would be happy to share your lab results. You should receive an email from Akute Health with instructions to access the portal. If you don't see it, please check your spam folder.
Akute Patient Portal - Email and Overview
When we send a patient an invite to the Patient Portal, they will receive an email from Akute with instructions.
Patient Point of View of Akute
Akute Patient Portal - Troubleshooting
Occasionally, patients have issues accessing the Patient Portal. These include, but are not limited to:
The 'sharing labs' option is unavailable and greyed out.
The patient is encountering an error when attempting to sign in.
The patient is unable to reset their password.
The labs do not display in the patient's portal account.
When these issues come up, we will need to contact Akute. Please tag Paytyn in Akute's Slack channel. They will need to diagnose the problem.
Internal Troubleshooting
Sometimes patients have issues accessing the portal.
If we have already invited a patient to the portal and the option is greyed out:
Send the Akute: Invite to Akute's Portal macro
We invited you to Akute's portal earlier. Did you receive an email from them? The email header should have been: Fountain Wellness - Patient Portal Code.
If the patient indicates that they cannot find the app/locate the email but we've sent them an invite:
Send the Akute: Download Akute's Portal macro
It looks like you may have signed up for Akute previously. You can download it via iOS (https://apps.apple.com/us/app/akute-patient-portal/id1571802525) and Android (https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.akutehealth.patientportal&pli=1) and click â€œForgot password to get back in.
Merging Akute Profiles
Instructional Video on Merging Patient Charts
Akute's Web Portal
â€‹
-Akute has a web portal that patients can access here: https://portal.akutehealth.com/login
-Some patients may prefer to use the web portal as opposed to the physical app, since it means they do not have to download anything.
-If patients have app-related issues (i.e the app not loading on their phone, closing randomly), we should suggest they use Akute's web portal.
â€‹
Secure Messaging
By default, patients only receive lab results through the Akute app. We can share documents with them securely by using the Conversation feature. We would prefer to share patient records through Akute as opposed to a third party messaging app since it is more secure, and most patients should already have the app downloaded.
Go to Conversations on Akute
Click +New Conversation
Select Secure Message
Click on Attach File and the files from the patientâ€™s documents section will load
Select the documents that you want to send to the patient
Click on Continue
Click on Send

Belmar WI
Belmar Pharmacy Workflow
Belmar Pharmacy Workflow
Overview
Point of Contact
If you have a general Belmar question/issue, please contact Jonathan Banks at Jonathan.banks@belmarpharma.com
If there is an patient or order-specific issue, please contact: clinicpaywi@belmarpharmacy.com
Operating Hours
The pharmacy operates from 8:00 AM to 5:00 PM Mountain time (Mondays to Fridays). Jonathan Banks may answer emails outside of those hours, but the pharmacy will only process orders during those hours.
Medications Prescribed
Belmar is no longer processing orders for TRT patients. We used to use them for Enclomiphene and Testosterone Cypionate, but due to their lengthy lead times, we transferred their prescriptions to other pharmacies.
They process most orders for HRT patients and can service 50 states.
Medications Prescribed: HRT
HRT Patients are prescribed topical and oral options for treatment. They can also subscribe to two add-on plans, Dessicated Thyroid and Spironolactone plans.
If the patient signs up for a one-month plan, the refills will be every 28 days. For the 3 or 12-month plan, all refills are at 84 days.
Belmar Portal
We can see the status of orders through Belmar's portal. You can log in to the portal using this link: Belmar Pharma Solutions. The log in info is:
Username: Tbragas
Password: Fountainteam!
Searching for Orders
Processing Refills
If the patient has a valid prescription with refills left on it, we can process the refill through Belmar's portal. Tickets are created on Intercom for this when the patient is due for a refill.
â€‹
From the sidebar, click on ezRefill Dispensing.
Please search for the patient who we need to process the order.
You can just select the prescriptions that need to be refilled.
Click on Use Last Patient Shipping Information to auto-populate the information for delivery/shipping information. Please double-check in the ticket to confirm that the last patient shipping information reflects their current status.
Confirm before submitting the refill, then click Submit.
Inform Belmar that we need to process an early refill. Please contact jonathan.banks@belmarpharma.com and clinicpaywi@belmarpharmacy.com and provide them with the order # we triggered and why we're processing the refill.
â€‹
Canceling Orders
Carrier
Belmar Pharmacy sends out all orders via UPS and does not require a signature. They are unable to deliver to third party locations (i.e CVS, Fedex, Walgreens),
Belmar processes orders typically within a week.
Tracking links are sent out automatically to patients once the order is with the carrier.
Replacement Orders
If a patient needs a replacement order, we will need to process it for them via Belmar's portal.
At this point, Belmar only wants a few people to be able to process refills on Belmar's portal. We are working on ways to address this. For the time being, if a patient has an issue with their order (e.g. they lose the shipment, or the vial breaks), we should send them the following macro and move the conversation to Tier II (for HRT or TRT): Orders: Processing Replacement
We'll work on processing a replacement for you as soon as possible at our own expense.
Early Refills
If an early refill is needed due to a dosage change, lost order, or other concerns, the Refills and Clarification team can contact Belmar to authorize the early refill.
Belmar can only process early refills 14 days before the patient's intended refill date due to the medication's best use date.
If a patient is traveling and asks for an extra order to account for travel, it would need to be within that period.
Updating a Patient's Address
We do not currently have an API system set up with Belmar, so the address reflected on Akute is not in their system. If a patient indicates they need to change their address, we will need to contact the relevant pharmacy branch.
The team member handling refills must cross-reference all patient addresses when submitting refills to make sure the medication goes to the correct address.
Expediting Orders
Belmar does not charge us for expediting orders, but they can only expedite orders in two situations.
A patient is out of medication.
If a script was processed or submitted incorrectly.
They will not expedite orders for any other reason. If we need to expedite an order for one of these reasons, please email and request a rush on the patient's order: jonathan.banks@belmarpharma.com
Overnight Shipping
Belmar charges us somewhere between $35-50 for overnight shipping. If a patient wants an order sent out with overnight shipping, we can send them the following macro: Belmar: Overnight shipping
We're happy to ask our partner pharmacy to send out the order with overnight shipping. We would need to run a one-time fee of $50 on your card for this to be initiated.
Please note that this pharmacy typically takes about a week to process orders, so although the shipping may be changed, it may still take a while for them to process the order. Please confirm that this is okay with you.
If the patient agrees, we must charge them $50 as a one-time payment and email jonathan.banks@belmarpharma.com and clinicpaywi@belmarpharmacy.com, requesting that they send the order out with overnight shipping.
Please note that if there are external delays/issues causing the shipment to be delayed, we can request Jonathan to send out orders with overnight shipping at no cost to the patient.
Order Clarification
Belmar occasionally sends us emails for orders that need clarification. These are routed to the 'Refills and Clarification' team on Intercom.
We will need to retrieve the order number from Belmar's portal, contact Belmar, and request clarification.
Please create a ticket for the provider with the necessary clarification if needed.

Other Processes
Cancellation Workflow (NEW!)
Cancellation Workflow (NEW!)
Overview
Patients may cancel subscriptions for several reasons, including: cost, lack of noticeable results, and/or choosing a local provider. Our goal is to allow patients to cancel without any friction. The more seamless the experience is, the more likely they will return.
Workflow for Cancellations
If a patient request to cancel: Go into stripe and cancel subscription. Create a ticket for refills and clarifications to update prescriptions on cancellation.
If a patient request a refund: Send ticket to shift supervisor. Shift Supervisor will review situation. If coordination with refills and clarification team is needed to confirm status of shipment we will coordinate from there.
If the reason for request was not already stated please confirm reason for cancellation request when we update patient on their cancellation being complete. This is to ensure the issue is not something that we could possible address with a different outcome.
â€‹
â€‹How to Cancel subscription on stripe
â€‹
If a patient wishes to cancel their subscription, please visit Stripe and cancel it. Please refer to the Stripe Workflow for more information.
â€‹
After canceling their subscription, please send them the macro: Billing: Cancel Subscription.
Understood. We'll cancel your subscription. Wishing you the best in your wellness journey.
Multiple Subscriptions
If a patient is on multiple subscriptions, please ask them to confirm what plan they would like to cancel using the macro: Cancel: Multiple Plans.
Okay - completely understood. Can you confirm which plans you want to cancel?
Side Effects
If a patient experiences side effects and wishes to cancel, please cancel their subscription and send them the macro titled "Cancel Side Effects."
Understood, we will cancel your subscription. Would you like to discuss your concerns further with the medical team?
If they want to discuss their medical concerns, please transfer the conversation over to the RNs. If they have canceled, they are still entitled to discuss their medical concerns, as they have paid for the subscription.
Please ensure they are canceled before you transfer them over to the RN team. We do not want them to be charged again if they have these concerns.
â€‹Failed Payments and Cancelations
A lot of patients who want to cancel their subscription respond to a failed payment message.
If they want to cancel their subscription, we should default to the usual process, but there are two additional steps.
We need to void the failed payment. To do this, go to the invoices on Stripe.
Click on the invoice for the failed payment and select the three dots on the right-hand side.
Click on 'change invoice status.' From here, click on 'Uncollectable.' We should not attempt to charge past-due invoices for patients who have canceled.
Once this is done, we need to unsubscribe them from failed payment messages.
Create a 'CS ticket', and in the title list 'unsubscribe from failed payment messages.' Please assign the ticket to the 'cio-unsubscribe team.'
Please ensure that you take these steps when cancelling patients. We do not want them to be charged after they cancelled their subscription.
â€‹
â€‹
Prorated Refunds
Patients on longer-term plans who cancel are eligible for prorated refunds if specifically requested. We can calculate the value for this based on the remaining subscription cost, minus the amount of medication they've received.
To calculate the refund amount, use the:
Refund Calculator
Enter the amount the patient paid.
Enter the number of weeks of medication dispensed (4ml vials equal 4 weeks).
Enter the total number of weeks paid.
The calculator will then give you the refund amount based on the details you input.
To process the partial refund:
1. Go to the patient's Stripe account.
2. Click Refund Payment.
3. Enter the refund amount in the designated field.
By default, Stripe processes a full refund, so be sure to adjust it for a partial refund.
If a patient cancels and is on a long-term subscription plan, send them the macro: Billing: Cancelling a termed plan + processing a refund
Billing: Cancelling a termed plan + processing a refund
We canceled your subscription and processed a refund of: to account for the unused duration of your subscription. Take care, and please text us if you want to resume treatment with us later. You won't need to complete the online evaluation again.
Cancelling Orders In Process
We should verify if an order is being processed for a patient who indicated they wanted to cancel.
If you see an order is in process on the sidebar, please create a ticket for refills and clarification so they can cancel the order.
Frequently Asked Questions (FAQs)
How would I resume treatment at a later date?
Cancel Subscription: Resume Treatment at a Later Date
If you'd like to resume treatment with us at a later date please text us. You'll need to schedule another visit with a provider to resume treatment with us.
What is your cancellation policy?
You can simply text us if you want to cancel. You would just need to let us know before your next billing cycle, otherwise the subscription will recur.
Is there any contract?
General CS: Is there a contract?
We do not have a contract. You can reach out at any point before your next scheduled subscription charge to cancel the service.
If I cancel partway through the subscription, what kind of refund would I receive?
Refunds: Terms for Longer Term Plans
If you are on a longer-term plan with us you would be refunded the remainder of the subscription. For instance, if you were on a 12-week plan and canceled 10 weeks into the plan we would process a refund equivalent to two weeks.
Can I get another blood test after canceling?
We can set your subscription to cancel at the end of your term if you'd like to cancel. If you're due for bloodwork before the end of the term, you will still be contacted to schedule bloodwork.
â€‹
â€‹

Internal CS Processes
Communication Style Guide
Communication Style Guide
Overview
Our goal is to have clear and effective communication with patients.
We are a telemedicine company - we are not selling couches. This is a service people subscribe to help them feel better and improve their health. We should guide them towards that goal!
We want to appear emphatic and human and make people feel heard to the best of our ability. We will not always be able to address every patient perfectly - but we should aim to do our best.
How to Start a Conversation
Use a proper greeting. It is important as it sets the tone for the whole conversation.
Greeting should be professional while remaining friendly and personal at the same time. For example, "Hello John, thank you for contacting Fountain TRT. How can I help you?"
If a patient starts the chat with the description of their problem, you can respond in the following language: "Hello John, I understand your concern and will be happy to help you. Let's see what I can do."
How to Ask Probing Questions
To avoid any confusion, ensure you understand the patient's issue before giving your instructions or offering solutions. There are many ways you can ask for clarification politely, such as the following:
Let me check if I have this right, you want me to...
If I understand you correctly...
Tell me more about...
So, you are saying that... Correct?
How to Say "I Don't Know"
If you don't know how to answer a patient's question, please communicate that appropriately to the patient. Here are some suggestions for proper communication etiquette:
That is a good question, let me find out for you.
I'm not sure but let me find out for you.
I will check with our medical providers and get back to you shortly.
Hold Etiquette
If you need some time to review a patient's information or research on a solution, ask for permission to place a patient on hold. You can say:
During a call, you can say: "Can I put you on hold for a moment?"
While texting patients, you can say: "Great, we will schedule your appointment shortly."
Make sure to thank the patient after receiving the consent and when you are back. You can say, "Thank you for waiting/holding."
If you feel that the resolution will take a longer time, you can inform the patient that we will get back to them soon.
How to Admit Fault
Be direct and clear about the fact that you made a mistake. If you made a mistake, say something like: "John, I'm sorry, I made a mistake and gave you the wrong pricing of the monthly plan. The correct price is..."
For example, "John, I'm sorry, we made a mistake and charged you with the wrong amount. We already processed a refund to your account."
In General
If we make a mistake, we should apologize and take responsibility. These are our errors, and they are within our control. It's going to happen from time to time, but we want to minimize these issues to whatever degree we can. Here are sample situations:
A message is missed
A provider made a mistake
We misread a patient's message
We charged the patient the wrong amount/made a billing mistake
For example, "John, I'm sorry, we made a mistake and charged you with the wrong amount. We already processed a refund to your account."
External Issues
We should avoid apologizing to patients as frequently as we do for issues that are outside of our control. Here are few examples:
If a patient is irate that Fedex lost their order rather than telling him: "Apologies for the inconvenience," we should tell them: "We'll reach out to Fedex and ask for an update on your order."
If Labcorp can't find their order rather than saying: "Apologies for the inconvenience", please say: "We're looking into this, and will make sure Labcorp gets what they need to take you in as soon as possible."
If Getlabs screws up (i.e. they're late, go to the wrong address...etc.), rather than telling them: "We apologize for the distress caused", we should tell them: "We're reaching out to Getlabs right now and will get this addressed as soon as possible."
If there is a pharmacy delay and a patient is irate rather than tell them: "We sincerely apologize for the delay", we should tell them: "We'll reach out to the pharmacy and get an update for you asap."
Terms We Should Avoid
Apologies for the inconvenience.
Apologies for the confusion.
I don't understand your question.
There's nothing we can do.
Calm down.
These terms typically trigger people and make an existing problem much worse. Instead, use the following terms:
That's very frustrating to deal with. We'll address that and make it right for you.
There was some miscommunication here. We'll get that addressed.
Can you please clarify your question?
It may be challenging to address this issue, but we'll do what we can.
We want to be able to assist you with this. I'm happy to connect with you, a manager, or a higher-up to address your issue if you'd like.
Lab Appointments Style Guide
When we should a patient at labcorp we should structure our response like the following:
Hi - we can schedule you at this location. They have availability at x times. Does that work for you?
Hi - this Labcorp location has availability at the following times: do any of these times work for you?
Hi - we checked for available time slots within your preferred parameters, but unfortunately, this location does not have availability during your preferred times. There is another location located at: would that work for you?
We need to avoid the following:
Referring to available time slots as 'an availability.'
Indicating that we have availability. We are not Labcorp, and patients may get confused. We have a billing arrangement with Labcorp, but they are not part of our company in any capacity.
Starting a sentence with 'apparently' or 'unfortunately.'
Rather than the sentence reading "Apparently there is an available time slot at..." or "Unfortunately there is no availability", please write the following:
There are openings at...
There is no availability during your preferred times, but we can schedule you at....
Grammar and Communication Guidelines
Use Proper Sentence Structure: Avoid overly complex sentences.
Instead of:
"Since we are still waiting for Labcorp to confirm your lab results, and it seems like it might take some time due to their current high volume of requests, we will update you as soon as we hear back from them and let you know if there are any further delays."
Say this:
"We are still waiting for Labcorp to confirm your lab results. Please be aware that this may take some time since Labcorp is backed up - but we will update you as soon as we hear back from them."
Avoid Over-Apologizing: Be solution-oriented.
Instead of:
"I'm really sorry about the inconvenience. Unfortunately, we can't do anything until we hear back from the pharmacy."
Say this:
"We'll reach out to the pharmacy and follow up with you as soon as we have more information."
Tone and Professionalism: Stay empathetic and professional.
Instead of:
"I'll get this sorted for you ASAP!"
Say this:
"I'll make sure this is resolved as soon as possible."
Consistent Terminology:
Use "our medical team" or "a member of our medical team" instead of "your provider" or "our providers".
Instead of:
"Your provider will review your lab results and reach out to you soon."
Say this:
"A member of our medical team will review your lab results and contact you soon."
Instead of:
"Our provider has availability this week."
Say this:
"Our medical team has availability this week."
This reinforces the team's collaborative nature and avoids confusion about whether a single provider or the entire medical team is involved.
Proper Punctuation: Correct use of commas and greetings.
Instead of:
"Hello John thank you for your patience we're working on it."
Say this:
"Hello, John. Thank you for your patience. We're working on it."
Avoid Negative Phrasing: Use positive alternatives.
Instead of:
"Unfortunately, we don't have any appointments available tomorrow."
Say this:
"There are no openings tomorrow, but we can offer you an appointment on Friday."
Capitalize Key Names and Titles: Ensure names, titles, and departments are capitalized.
Instead of:
"A member of our medical team will handle this for you, victor will review your results."
Say this:
"A member of our medical team, Victor, will review your results."
Responding to Patient Question: Acknowledge the concern before providing the solution.
Instead of:
"You should receive your order within 3-5 business days."
Say this:
"I understand you're concerned about your order. You should receive it within 3-5 business days."
Acknowledge Problems and work on creating solutions:
We should always strive to acknowledge mistakes while simultaneously seeking solutions.
Instead of:
"Apologies for the inconvenience - I'm sure that was frustrating"
Say this:
"We hear you - and we'll make this right for you."
Recommended Tools for Grammar & Spelling
Macros
Most general communication is done through macros. The macros are handy to answer process questions and handle day-to-day tasks.
90% of communication can be done through macro variation - but not 100%. If we misuse macros, communication will appear robotic.
If someone is upset with shipping times, it's not helpful for us to tell them that it will take up to eight days for their order to arrive.
In these cases, we should tell them that we understand their frustration and will work on getting their order out as soon as possible. We do not need to give them a specific timeline.
Misunderstandings and Examples
Third Party Issues
Occasionally, patients misunderstand due to a third party or a preconceived notion. We should correct that politely and pleasantly.
We can check our internal documentation to confirm whether what they are saying is accurate.
In this case, the guy is confused (albeit for unclear reasons.)
We can tell them something to the effect of:
Happy to help you with this. We do operate in your state. We'll check our website to make sure it's up to date. We'd love to work with you if you're interested in moving forward. If not, we can certainly refund your assessment fee.
Subscription Fees/Pricing
Patients sometimes are not aware of what their subscription fees are.
In this case, the patient is not particularly annoyed - but he is misremembering how much he's charged.
We can tell them something to the effect of:
Happy to help with this. Your subscription is run at $897 every 12 weeks. You can see past payments here: (billing portal link). If you have any questions, please let us know. Hope you have a great day!
Sometimes, people are upset with our pricing.
In this case, this guy used to pay $50 for tadalafil with us. He's not an active patient anymore. He felt as though we were overcharging him.
In these cases, we can tell him we appreciate his feedback and wish him the best. We don't need to argue with him over what we charge versus competitors. It doesn't serve any value.

Internal CS Processes
Communication Style Guide
Communication Style Guide
Overview
Our goal is to have clear and effective communication with patients.
We are a telemedicine company - we are not selling couches. This is a service people subscribe to help them feel better and improve their health. We should guide them towards that goal!
We want to appear emphatic and human and make people feel heard to the best of our ability. We will not always be able to address every patient perfectly - but we should aim to do our best.
How to Start a Conversation
Use a proper greeting. It is important as it sets the tone for the whole conversation.
Greeting should be professional while remaining friendly and personal at the same time. For example, "Hello John, thank you for contacting Fountain TRT. How can I help you?"
If a patient starts the chat with the description of their problem, you can respond in the following language: "Hello John, I understand your concern and will be happy to help you. Let's see what I can do."
How to Ask Probing Questions
To avoid any confusion, ensure you understand the patient's issue before giving your instructions or offering solutions. There are many ways you can ask for clarification politely, such as the following:
Let me check if I have this right, you want me to...
If I understand you correctly...
Tell me more about...
So, you are saying that... Correct?
How to Say "I Don't Know"
If you don't know how to answer a patient's question, please communicate that appropriately to the patient. Here are some suggestions for proper communication etiquette:
That is a good question, let me find out for you.
I'm not sure but let me find out for you.
I will check with our medical providers and get back to you shortly.
Hold Etiquette
If you need some time to review a patient's information or research on a solution, ask for permission to place a patient on hold. You can say:
During a call, you can say: "Can I put you on hold for a moment?"
While texting patients, you can say: "Great, we will schedule your appointment shortly."
Make sure to thank the patient after receiving the consent and when you are back. You can say, "Thank you for waiting/holding."
If you feel that the resolution will take a longer time, you can inform the patient that we will get back to them soon.
How to Admit Fault
Be direct and clear about the fact that you made a mistake. If you made a mistake, say something like: "John, I'm sorry, I made a mistake and gave you the wrong pricing of the monthly plan. The correct price is..."
For example, "John, I'm sorry, we made a mistake and charged you with the wrong amount. We already processed a refund to your account."
In General
If we make a mistake, we should apologize and take responsibility. These are our errors, and they are within our control. It's going to happen from time to time, but we want to minimize these issues to whatever degree we can. Here are sample situations:
A message is missed
A provider made a mistake
We misread a patient's message
We charged the patient the wrong amount/made a billing mistake
For example, "John, I'm sorry, we made a mistake and charged you with the wrong amount. We already processed a refund to your account."
External Issues
We should avoid apologizing to patients as frequently as we do for issues that are outside of our control. Here are few examples:
If a patient is irate that Fedex lost their order rather than telling him: "Apologies for the inconvenience," we should tell them: "We'll reach out to Fedex and ask for an update on your order."
If Labcorp can't find their order rather than saying: "Apologies for the inconvenience", please say: "We're looking into this, and will make sure Labcorp gets what they need to take you in as soon as possible."
If Getlabs screws up (i.e. they're late, go to the wrong address...etc.), rather than telling them: "We apologize for the distress caused", we should tell them: "We're reaching out to Getlabs right now and will get this addressed as soon as possible."
If there is a pharmacy delay and a patient is irate rather than tell them: "We sincerely apologize for the delay", we should tell them: "We'll reach out to the pharmacy and get an update for you asap."
Terms We Should Avoid
Apologies for the inconvenience.
Apologies for the confusion.
I don't understand your question.
There's nothing we can do.
Calm down.
These terms typically trigger people and make an existing problem much worse. Instead, use the following terms:
That's very frustrating to deal with. We'll address that and make it right for you.
There was some miscommunication here. We'll get that addressed.
Can you please clarify your question?
It may be challenging to address this issue, but we'll do what we can.
We want to be able to assist you with this. I'm happy to connect with you, a manager, or a higher-up to address your issue if you'd like.
Lab Appointments Style Guide
When we should a patient at labcorp we should structure our response like the following:
Hi - we can schedule you at this location. They have availability at x times. Does that work for you?
Hi - this Labcorp location has availability at the following times: do any of these times work for you?
Hi - we checked for available time slots within your preferred parameters, but unfortunately, this location does not have availability during your preferred times. There is another location located at: would that work for you?
We need to avoid the following:
Referring to available time slots as 'an availability.'
Indicating that we have availability. We are not Labcorp, and patients may get confused. We have a billing arrangement with Labcorp, but they are not part of our company in any capacity.
Starting a sentence with 'apparently' or 'unfortunately.'
Rather than the sentence reading "Apparently there is an available time slot at..." or "Unfortunately there is no availability", please write the following:
There are openings at...
There is no availability during your preferred times, but we can schedule you at....
Grammar and Communication Guidelines
Use Proper Sentence Structure: Avoid overly complex sentences.
Instead of:
"Since we are still waiting for Labcorp to confirm your lab results, and it seems like it might take some time due to their current high volume of requests, we will update you as soon as we hear back from them and let you know if there are any further delays."

Misc
Intermingled Patient Information
Intermingled Patient Information
Overview:
-Our system requires each patient to have their own account. They cannot share accounts with other patients.
-While this seems straightforward enough, some patients in our care try to get their partners or family members to register using the same email. This overwrites the existing profiles' information and leads to significant problems.
-This leads to multiple different problems:
1. It is a HIPAA violation since they can see prior exchanges with the patient on Akute/access their lab results.
2. This means that prescriptions cannot be sent adequately since the patient's information on file does not match the information being prescribed.
â€‹
â€‹
Discovery:
-There are multiple ways we can discover that a patient has intermingled information. The two most common are:
1) They mention their partner is having issues connecting to a visit.
2) A ticket is created by the medical team indicating they saw the wrong patient during the visit.
3) We see the wrong lab orders/lab results on their chart
â€‹
â€‹
â€‹Fixing the Issue:
-If we discover a patient has intermingled information, we can send the macro: 'Intermingled Profiles.'
Hello, It looks like your profile has intermingled information for you and someone else who may have used the same email (likely a family member.) While we're glad that you're both interested in our programs, we would need you both to have separate profiles. You'll need to complete the assessment for us to decouple the profiles. We can refund the additional assessment fees paid upon re-registering - you'd just need to let us know after registering. Please let us know if you have any questions.
-Once the patient confirms, they will need to complete the assessment and will need to let us know.
-We will need to decouple the information from their charts. To start with, the best option would be to go off the original lab order/results on the patient's chart, and update the Akute profile for the patient with that info.
-> Once done, the patient should be able to register using their unique information and move forward.
â€‹
â€‹
â€‹
â€‹

Compliance Workflow
Compliance Workflow
Overview
Fountain Vitality is a telemedicine company that must comply with local and federal laws.
Each state has laws in the book, which causes slight deviations in how scripts are written/processed.
While CS does not write scripts ourselves, we must abide by federal and local laws and coordinate with our medical team to make sure we comply.
Video Visits
For compliance purposes, we ask all new patients (TRT, HRT, and GLP), to have video visits with our medical team, not phone calls. Our medical team can review the lab results with the patient without seeing them on camera, but we cannot prescribe TRT to them unless they appear on camera.
We can also technically prescribe non-controlled substances (e.g., enclomphine, tadalafil, anastrozole, and GLP medications) without a video visit. However, we still prefer all patients to get a video visit so we can get a better picture of their health profile, even if they arenâ€™t on controlled substances.
Controlled Substances
Most TRT or HRT program patients are prescribed TRT (Testosterone Replacement Therapy). Testosterone is a controlled substance and has certain restrictions in place, including, but not limited to:
A patient must appear on camera during a video visit to be prescribed.
A video visit is required for each new or refill script submitted to a pharmacy.
Take Note:
Please note that if a prescription is written for a pharmacy but the order does not go out, the prescription can be submitted to a different pharmacy or modified.
Please note that Enclomphine (sometimes called oral TRT), is not a controlled substance.
Prescription Refills (TRT)
If a patient needs a TRT refill, our system will send them a message 30 days before it is due, indicating they must schedule a video visit to process it.
This message is sent out based on the following parameters:
The patient's subscription is active, and they do not have failed payments on their account.
The patient's prescription fill date is 30 days away, but the system detects no refills on file.
Non-controlled substances
Patients on ED meds or Enclomiphene do not technically need video visits for refills, but they receive the same messages for now. We're working on getting an asynchronous form set up for these patients, which they'll fill out to receive their refill.
Video Visit and Refills
Each controlled substance script or refill needs a video visit to be processed unless the previous order did not go out. This includes even when a script is sent to a local pharmacy, and they need a new script sent to one of our partner pharmacies. Any new RX will require a video visit - even if it seems arbitrary.
Most prescriptions are submitted with refills good for about 6 months, although this varies based on specific state guidelines. Some states, like California, require scripts to be written with fewer refills, which means patients will need more frequent video visits.
If a patient indicates they want to switch treatment and would like an order sent to a local pharmacy because they are concerned about delivery times or anything requiring a new controlled substance script, they would need a new video visit.
Switching Treatment Options
Each controlled substance prescription needs an associated video visit to get processed.
If a patient wants to switch to injections or cream after an order has already been processed since, they need a new video visit.
If they want to switch to Enclomphine, they don't need a new video visit since it's not a controlled substance.
HCG and Compliance
HCG is considered a controlled substance in some states - and by some pharmacies.
Curexa does not consider it a controlled substance in the same way they do TRT, and as such, does not have a signature requirement for it (unlike TRT.) They require early refills be treated the same way as controlled substances would be for HCG.
We generally require patients to have video visits if they need an HCG refill since it is technically a controlled substance in certain states.
Refills for Non-controlled Substances
Our system sends messages to patients who need refills for other types of medication and asks them to schedule a video visit. This process will be changed soon, but there are a few nuances with non-controlled substances.
The provider who wrote the initial RX does not need to see the patient during a video visit. However, they will probably write refills on the RX after seeing the patient during a video visit. A different provider in our practice can technically write the script for a patient, even if they haven't seen them before. We prefer to avoid this when we can, but it's not a legal requirement.
The initial visit for the patient to write these RXs does not need to be a video visit - it can be a phone call. That being said, all initial visits are done over video since the patient may want to start a controlled substance medication.
Cancelling and Refunding Patients
We're working on a system that will create tickets for patients due for refills but never schedule the visit.
We will send them multiple manual follow-ups a few days before their refill is due.
If the patient does not respond after several of these, we will cancel their subscription.
Until this is set up, though, we may occasionally encounter patients upset that they were charged but have not had orders sent. Since no order went out, we can refund these patients if they request it.
The solution is to cancel before that becomes necessary preemptively, but this is just a makeshift solution.
PMP Conflicts
Typically, the MAs (medical assistants) review patients' controlled substance prescriptions and the PMP (Prescription Monitoring Program). They or the provider will message the patient indicating that we submitted a new prescription - but it can't be processed since they had a prescription filled by another provider/pharmacy.
Controlled substances can only be filled in certain intervals due to government regulation. This is not the case for non-controlled substance prescriptions.
Occasionally, this is either missed or due to logistical/order delays, and it's not caught until the pharmacy reaches out.
If a pharmacy mentions that they had an order filled by another pharmacy/provider recently, please ask which pharmacy filled it. We do this for two reasons:
So that we can tell the patient which pharmacy filled the order (they may have filled it alongside other medications and forgotten).
To make sure the pharmacy has the correct information. They occasionally make mistakes and get mixed up.
Once the pharmacy confirms the name of the pharmacy that processed their order, please send the patient the macro:
If a patient had an order processed by another pharmacy recently
Hi - our pharmacy partner indicated that you had a TRT order filled by: on:
Our providers can only prescribe if you're not filling other prescriptions for the same medication since this is a controlled substance. We would love to provide your treatment but need confirmation from you that we're going to be your only medical provider for TRT.
We need your confirmation before the pharmacy can process this order. Please note that they may not be able to process it immediately since another RX was by an outside pharmacy. Thank you.
If the patient confirms they filled it at another pharmacy but wants to proceed, we should ask our partner pharmacy when they can fill the order.
Once we get their confirmation, we should relay that information to the patient.
If they are a new patient and have not received medication from us, we should adjust their charge date.
It may make sense if they are an existing patient, but please review the context and/or see if the patient is asking for a change in their charge first.
If they indicate they will be using another provider and would like to cancel their subscription, please cancel it.
Handling Non-Controlled Prescriptions for Patients of Former Providers
This section is for handling prescriptions for patients whose previous provider is no longer affiliated with Fountain.
Patients on Enclomiphene, Anastrozole and ED meds are not required to see a provider in a video visit since these medications don't have a controlled substance.
Providers don't need to have seen the patient before submitting the prescription. Any provider licensed in the patient's state can send the refill.
If a provider is uncomfortable writing for the patient due to not having seen them, we can schedule a video visit with the provider and then they can prescribe.
Steps to follow
Confirm Patient's state
Check the Provider Licensing by State sheet
â€‹Please refer to this document and select the TRT sheet to view the list of providers and their state licenses: Provider Licensing by State / Availability Report
Create a ticket to submit the prescription and assign it to the provider.
If a provider saw a patient several months ago and is no longer with us, but the patient wants to start treatment, they will need a new video visit. Please send them the macro:
VV: Provider no longer works with us - New VV is needed
Hello, I reached out to our medical team, and they indicated that the provider you saw earlier is no longer with us. Sorry for the hassle, but you'll need to schedule a new video visit for compliance purposes to get this prescription processed.
If they want a refund due to the inconvenience, please refund them since no order was processed for them.
They do not need a new video visit if they are starting Enclomphine or another non-controlled substance (please refer to the last section).
Extra Medication Requests
Some patients ask for extra medication. Sometimes, this is due to concerns of running out early; other times, it's due to travel/extenuating circumstances.
If it's a non-controlled substance, these can usually be filled as long as an order was not filled for the patient recently.
If it's a controlled substance, it becomes more complicated, but we can fill it through our partner pharmacies. If there is a good reason for it, we can fill the order - but we can't without justification.
If the medication is sent out through Curexa, we must go through the early refill process.
If the medication is sent through another pharmacy, we would need to justify the refill and ask the pharmacy to process the order for the patient.
Shipping Delays and Extra Medication
It becomes quite tricky if a patient is upset due to a shipping delay for an order that has already gone out and asks for the prescription to be filled locally.
The prescription could be filled at a local pharmacy without issues if the order has not gone. You can ask the provider to submit the RX to a local pharmacy since the PMP was checked recently. The ticket would need to go to the MAs.
If a patient asks about it, please create a ticket for Joshua Faucett. He will address the situation.
In most cases, we can't fill the order at a local pharmacy since an order has already been processed for the patient by another pharmacy.
Prescription Refills (GLP)
The RX Tasks board is used by providers to monitor prescription statuses for all active patients.
Tasks are only marked as complete if the patient cancels; they are kept open, and due dates are moved to the patient's next refill date.
Due date of the RX Task - This is when the provider sends the prescription refill request to the pharmacy. Providers sometimes submit refills early to account for pharmacy processing time.
Restarting Treatment
Fountain TRT
If a patient cancels their TRT or HRT subscription after more than two months, they will need a new video visit to write a new prescription.
Patients who were previously on Enclomphine should still have a new video visit.
If they are on ED-only plans, they do not need a new video visit since it's not a controlled substance.
Fountain GLP
If a patient who has canceled their membership decides to restart their treatment, we offer an option for them to do so. To restart their treatment, they need to schedule a call with a provider. The reason for this is to discuss and potentially adjust the patient's dosage, especially if there was a break in their treatment.
If the last prescription was sent within the past month, use the provider follow-up link.
If the last prescription was sent over a month ago, use the initial video visit link.
Licensing
All of our providers have licenses in certain states. None of them are licensed in all 50 states. A patient needs to see a licensed provider in their state to be prescribed and receive medical advice.
You can find the list of the providers and what type of patients they see here: Provider Licensing by State / Availability Report
This sheet gets updated when providers gain new licenses/have their licenses expire in certain states.
State Issues for Controlled Substances
We are technically licensed and able to operate in the following states, but they have restrictions that make it inconvenient for patients to get care:
Kentucky:
They would require an MD or DO to submit TRT scripts with refills. This means only Doron Stember or Joshua Stadler could see the patients.
Connecticut :
Requires an in-person visit for the initial prescription.
Georgia:
Requires an in-person visit for the initial prescription.
Alabama :
Requires an in-person visit for the initial prescription.
Missouri:
Requires a physical office space for controlled substances to be submitted. We are able to prescribe Enclomphine and ED meds. Unlike the other states, we allow patients to register from Missouri.
We are able to prescribe Enclomphine or ED meds to patients in all of these states, but we do not allow new patients to register from these states. (Click here to view the map showing Fountain's Active States)
We do have patients in these states who have met providers in person to adhere to these rules or who will only be seen by Doron in Kentucky.
New RX/Refill Assignment
In general, whenever a new controlled substance prescription is needed and the patient has already had their visit, a ticket should be created and assigned to the MAs.
They will check the PMP (the prescription monitoring program), to confirm that the patient is not getting prescribed through another provider.
Once the PMP has been checked, they will assign the ticket to the provider who last saw the patient.
In general - we should avoid asking the providers to send over a new controlled substance prescription.
The one exception is if a prescription is written with an error/kicked back to us and needs clarification. In these cases, we can ask the provider to address the error/resubmit the script since the PMP has already been checked.
Any other medication script (i.e., tadalafil, anastrozole) can go directly to the provider who saw the patient last. If that provider is no longer with us/unavailable, it is transferred over to another provider licensed in the patient's state. It does not need to go to the MAs since it is not a controlled substance.
Common Questions and Issues
Some patients want to see a specific provider that does not come up when they try to book the visit. If they want to see a specific provider after we (or the system), send out the state-based scheduling link. We can send them the macro:
VV: Request for a specific provider
No problem. Our system defaults to the state-based link and gives you the option to schedule the soonest available visit. If you'd like to see a specific provider that's no problem though. You can use this link to schedule with them:
Take Note:
Please review the state licensing document to make sure the provider is licensed in the patient's state before sending the snippet. They may have moved, and the provider they saw previously may not be licensed there.
There are one or two states where we only have one or two providers. If we do not have another provider licensed in that state, we must let the patient know that we don't have any other providers licensed in their state.
If a patient indicates they travel between two states frequently, we should try our best to get them a licensed provider in those states.
If we do not have a provider licensed in both states, we will need to let them know that we do not have a provider licensed in one of those states. They will need to see a provider/have medication shipped to the state we are licensed in.
Manual Follow-Ups (Controlled Substance Refills Only)
If patients don't schedule visits to get controlled substance refills, we cannot process their RXes. Our system sends out multiple messages, but cannot force them to schedule a visit.
We have a communication flow set up if you're assigned a ticket to send a follow-up message to a patient indicating they need a new video visit.
Please check to see if the patient scheduled their video visit during each process step. If the patient has scheduled their video visit, please snooze the ticket until the day after the scheduled visit.
f they have scheduled the visit, please complete the ticket. If not, we will need to send them the rescheduling link.
Send the VV link
VV: Visit required before refill 1
Hi - you're due for a refill. You'll need to speak with a provider in a quick video visit to process the prescription. Please use this link to schedule your visit:
After sending the first message, please use the snooze submission button to snooze the ticket for 48 hours.
Send follow-up messages every 48 hours
Each time the conversation is reopened, send a follow-up message then snooze the conversation for 48 hours.
VV: Visit required before refill 2
Hi - this is Fountain. You're overdue for refill. You'll need to speak with a provider in a quick video visit to process the prescription. Please use this link to schedule your visit:
VV: Visit required before refill 3
Hi - this is Fountain. We're just following up with you again. You're overdue for refill. You'll need to speak with a provider in a quick video visit to process the prescription. Please use this link to schedule your visit:
VV: Visit required before refill 4
Hi - this is Fountain. We're just following up with you again. We've sent you a few texts indicating that you're due for a refill - but that you need to speak a provider to get it processed. Please use this link to schedule your visit:
Send a follow-up email on the 5th attempt
VV: Visit required before refill 5
Hi - we're just following up with you again. To get your refill processed you'll need to schedule a quick video visit with a provider. Please use this link to schedule the visit:
After that message, we should use the snooze submission button again.
Send the cancellation message
Once the ticket is reopened, we should send them the following macro and cancel their subscription.
Cancel Subscription: Overdue for refill
Hi - this is Fountain. As mentioned earlier, you're due for a refill but need to speak with a provider in a quick visit for this to be processed. We've sent you several texts, emailed you, but we haven't been able to connect with you.
We will need to cancel your subscription since we can't get your refill processed until you speak with a provider. Wishing you the best.
Frequently Asked Questions (FAQs)
Why does it need to be a video visit?
â€‹For any testosterone-related visits, a video visit is required for compliance purposes.
Do I really need a video visit to process my refill?
â€‹Our medical team has indicated that they need to see patients in a video visit to write a refill for compliance reasons.
Other Compliance Questions
If a compliance or legal question is not explicitly covered in this document, we should create a ticket for Joshua Faucett, who heads up the medical operations team.

Critical Escalations Flow
Critical Escalations Flow
Note: Please use this workflow in any situation that seems serious. It doesn't have to be limited to the issues mentioned above. Please use your judgment.
Escalate Immediately to Doron/Tzvi if Legal Action is mentioned:
Accusation of Medical Malpractice
Lawyer
Mention of a lawsuit/legal action
For the above examples please do the following:
Cease all communication
Create a ticket for the Shift Supervisor Team, star as Priority (Ticket Type: Shift Supervisor, Team Inbox: Shift Supervisor, Assignee: will load balance accordingly).
The Shift Supervisor will then send a WhatsApp message to the critical escalations channel tagging Doron, Tzvi, and Team Lead Provider.
In Intercom shift supervisor will reassign both the ticket and conversation to:
-Team Inboxâ†’ Critical Patient Issues
-Assignee â†’ Doron
Tag both Tzvi, Doron, and Team Lead Provider
Monitor to ensure the matter is addressed. If no acknowledgement within 30 minutes-1 hour send a message to Camryn and Lindsay.

Chest pain, left arm pain, or jaw pain
Shortness of breath
Vision loss (one or both eyes) on any medication/Visual disturbances on Enclomiphene
Unilateral calf pain
Sudden loss of balance, speech difficulty, or weakness in part of the body (including facial droop)
Loss of consciousness
Severe nausea or vomiting
Severe abdominal pain
Female patients- excessive vaginal bleeding
Threats of physical harm (to self or others)
Critical lab results from a lab call
Lab calling with ANY critical lab results.
Action:

Internal Tracker Tickets
Internal Tracker Tickets
Overview: The medical team creates internal tracker tickets for patients that cannot be found.
The vast majority of these patients register for visits using another name. Sometimes it's as simple as a typo, so when they put in the patient's name on Akute they don't come up.
â€‹
Process:
-If you receive one of these tickets(showcased below) please look at the provider's google calendar for the specified date and time.
-You then can take the email listed on the google calendar booking and search in intercom by selecting the magnifying glass in the top left corner of intercom. If not located via intercom the email can also be plugged into Stripe and Oncehub.
-Please then locate patient in Akute, and update profile accordingly. Ensure you paste the Akute profile link into the ticket, and reassign the ticket to the provider scheduled to see the patient.
-If we are unable to locate the patient by searching their email in Intercom, Stripe, and Oncehub please call the number listed on the calendar invite. It is possible that the patient registered using a different email so we would need to call the patient to confirm.
â€‹
If searching in Stripe this image indicates where you would see the email listed.

Curexa
Curexa Pharmacy Issues & Early Refills
Curexa Pharmacy Issues & Early Refills
Overview
Curexa Pharmacy is Fountain's longest-withstanding pharmacy partner. They've been with us since 2021. They process TRT cream and injections, anastrozole, sildenafil, HCG, and oral semaglutide.
Curexa primarily operates over email. If there is a patient issue or an order needs clarification, we should contact their partner support team at: partnersupport@curexa.helpscoutapp.com
They do not have a portal, but we have an API system. Each order is triggered by an API call which is transmitted to their system. They would prefer we use OIDs (from retool), rather than patient information for HIPAA compliance purposes.
Why Curexa Contacts Us
Curexa Pharmacy sends an email to inform us about any concerns regarding a patient's order. The email contains information about the situation with the order and provides instructions to ensure that the patient receives their prescriptions.
Some examples of possible issues include:
Missing Electronic Prescription
Order being canceled due to a missing electronic prescription, requiring a new script to be provided
Expired Prescription
Order being canceled due to an expired prescription, requiring a new script to be provided in order to re-process the order
No Refills Remaining
Order being canceled due to a "No refills remaining" error, requiring a new script to be provided in order to re-process the order
Refill Too Soon
Order being rejected due to an early refill (possible result of duplicate orders)
Handling Curexa Emails
Here are some sample emails that you may receive from Curexa:
When you get assigned to handle a Curexa email, please do the following:
Go to Retool and search for the patient using the Order ID (OID) or Patient ID (PID) that sent by Curexa. Scroll down to view the patient's information and use them to look up the patient on Akute.
Visit the patient's Akute Notes and review the most recent Progress Notes to determine the assigned provider for the patient.
Create a ticket for the provider on Intercom indicating the order issue.
Create a new back-office ticket
Indicate the Curexa order issue
Assign the ticket to the provider
Set the Due Date to current date
Respond to Curexa.
Tracking Links
When Curexa orders go out, a tracking link is automatically sent to the patient. We want to set this up with our other partner pharmacies and are actively working on that. The tracking link should also appear in the sidebar on Intercom.
Expediting Orders
If a patient requests to expedite shipping, please do the following:
1. Respond to the patient
Please send the Curexa: Delayed, need to expedite order macro:
Hey - I just checked on this and I can see the pharmacy is still working on your order. We'll reach out to the pharmacy that is shipping your order and ask them to expedite it. Have a great rest of your day!
2. Locate the order ID (OID) on Retool
Click Patient Dashboard in the patientâ€™s Akute profile to be redirected to Retool
Scroll down and navigate to Shipments
If order is eligible for expedited shipping, get the order ID (OID).
3. Send an email request to Curexa
Please send the Shipping: Email request to expedite order macro to partnersupport@curexa.helpscoutapp.com and indicate the order ID.
Hello,
Please expedite order ID:
â€‹Thank you.
Processing Overnight Delivery
If patients request an order to be sent overnight or are anxious about their order and willing to pay for a faster delivery, we can ask the pharmacy to process the order via FedEx overnight delivery.
Assuming the patient is not angry at us, or their order was delayed due to our issues, please send the Orders: Overnight Shipping macro:
We will ask the pharmacy to expedite the order. We can also ask them to process the delivery via overnight shipping. We would need to charge your account a one-time $50 payment for this to be initiated. Please note that this doesn't guarantee that it will go out the next day, but we will ask our partner pharmacy to do their best. Is that okay with you?
If the patient accepts:
If the patient declines but is okay with expedited shipping only:
Extra Medication for Traveling
Patients sometimes request extra medication for travel purposes.
They cannot ship outside the United States (barring US military bases), but they can process additional medication for traveling patients.
The best approach is for the patient to have a video visit since they may need additional refills to process this request. We can send them the General CS: Extra medication for travel macro:
Our partner pharmacy, Curexa, would potentially be able to process additional medication for you. You may need a new script to get this processed. For compliance purposes, you'll need to schedule a new visit to get this processed. Here's the link:
Once the new script is processed, we need to run a $75 charge to process the additional medication for you.
Once they have had the visit the provider should create a ticket indicating that they will need an early refill for travel. We can send the patient the Pharmacy: Extra Meds Travel 1 macro:
Hi - I understand you'll be traveling soon. We'll ask our partner pharmacy to process an early refill for you, as well as asking them to send out extra meds for travel. We'll just need to run a $75 charge to get this processed for you. Please let us know if that works for you.
Once they agree, we should run the one-time charge and send out the early refill request to Curexa.
Pharmacy Issues
Mixed Up Patient Orders
Orders Filled Incorrectly
Damages and Defects
Incorrect Vial Size
Early Refills
What is an Early Refill for?
Communication
Early Refill Authorization
Common Messages to Curexa
T Cyp for North Carolina Patients
Curexa cannot process T cyp refills for patients in North Carolina. They can technically process the original RX - but due to a logistical issue, they cannot process refills.
If an RX is sent to Curexa, our system will still trigger it, but Curexa will return this error message.
The patient will need a new RX submitted to another pharmacy. They may need a new visit to submit the RX.
Things to Note
In case there is an error on Akute (such as Needs refills, Missing electronic prescription, Prescription expired), we should send a direct message to the provider on WhatsApp. The pharmacy cannot proceed with the order until that error is resolved.
We might have already requested Curexa to expedite the order. You can verify this by reviewing previous texts with the patient. If that is the case, we need to follow up with Curexa. Please send this message to the patient and ask Curexa for an update:
"We apologize for the delay - we will contact Curexa for an update."
If a refill went out within the last ten days, our system has an automatic check to prevent a new API call from going out.
If you need to process an early refill that needs to be filled before those ten days are up, we need to click on this checkbox:

Medical
Extra Medication
Extra Medication
Overview
Our goal is to give patients enough medication until their next refill.
Occasionally, patients receive extra medication for one of the following reasons:
Reduced dosage
Pharmacy Error
Two orders are being placed simultaneously from different pharmacies
CS error
Provider error
It is in our best interest to avoid this, but it occasionally happens.
Steps
If a patient indicates they have extra medication and it's not apparent why, we should ask the medical team to confirm that they are dosing correctly.
Once the medical team responds, they will let us know if we need to do anything or if it was the patient's error and will be handled by them.
3. If they have extra medication, we may need to skip medication refills. There are two separate processes for this (see below.)
Add-on plans
If a patient has extra HCG, tadalafil, anastrozole, or hair loss medication, we can send them the macro: Extra Meds: Add-on cancel
Hello, I understand that you have extra: We can cancel your add-on plan for now to account for that. Please let us know when you have about two weeks of medication left so our medical team can submit a new RX and so that we can reenable your subscription. The previous one may have expired by then. Please let us know if you have any questions!
Please cancel their subscription. They don't need to pay for the subscription, as it just covers the cost of medication.
Subscription Plans
Pausing/Skipping Shipments
1. Pause Shipments
If a patient requests to pause due to excess medication:
Create a ticket for the RN Team.
RN team confirms correct dosing.
If dosing is correct and there are no expired-med concerns, the RN defers to the Refills and Clarifications team via ticket. They will adjust next shipment due date.
We will not pause if supply will be needed in one month or less.
If a patient request to pause due to different reason:
-Create a ticket for Refills and Clarifications team.
-They will evaluate if plausible reason to adjust refill date/pause shipments.
Notes
Subscriptions remain active; billing continues because services are bundled (product, support, visits, and labs in most states).
Macro â€“ Pause Confirmation (send to patient):
Hello from Fountain!
I understand you have extra medication that will last you until [insert date]. To ensure you continue receiving regular labs, monitoring, support, and consultations, your plan will remain active. However, we can have our partner pharmacy hold your medication shipments until youâ€™re ready for more.
Since your shipments are paused, your prescription will expire when you are next due for a refill. Please reach out when you have about two weeks of medication left so we can unpause your shipments. If you are on testosterone, youâ€™ll also need to complete a new video visit so a provider can write a new prescription. This lead time helps make sure you donâ€™t run out of medication.
We have manually paused your current medications, so no refills will be sent while your account is on pause. Please note that pausing your medication refills does not pause your subscription, as the plan includes bundled services such as follow-up visits and routine monitoring labs in most states.
If you have any questions or are ready to resume shipments, just let us know â€” weâ€™re happy to help!
Pausing and unpausing medication shipments
â€‹ Pausing Shipments (Refills and Clarifications Only!)
-Patients subscribe to a subscription plan which covers the cost of medication, shipping, consultation, taxes, support, and labs (in most states.) Some patients may have extra medication for the reasons below and want to 'pause their subscription.' We cannot do this:
1) They have been underdosing and accumulating extra medication due to this.
2) They have been traveling and have not used their medication as prescribed.
-CS/Billing will handle the communication and credit for these patients. Refills and Clarifications team will pause shipments for the patient retool.
-If we need to pause patients' shipments, we should go into retool and look at 'paused medication bands.'
-From here, we select the medication we are pausing.
-Most names are straightforward, but some, like testosterone cream for HRT patients, are odd and display as 'HRT-T.' If you have questions about the medication bands, please reach out to Daniel.
-Once the band is paused, you'll see that the patient's shipments are paused on Intercom/Akute.
â€‹
-This will cause no refill tickets to be generated by the system, nor will any api calls for the paused medication go out.
-What we should do to make sure we get the patient handled correctly is to create a ticket for the intended date the patient should be unpaused for and unsnooze for that date.
-Once the ticket has been reopened, we need to resume shipments and manually create a refill ticket/request so that the order is processed for the patient as intended.
