Internal article
LabCorp
Labcorp Workflow
Labcorp Workflow
Overview
Most patients in our care are scheduled at Labcorp. Most patients manually book these visits themselves using a link sent out to them when they are either due for labs or after completing the evaluation.
Most patients use Quest instead of Labcorp, as we do not cover the lab fees at Labcorp. We charge patients $37 for Labcorp appointments, outside of non-client billing states (where they use insurance to pay for the lab visit.)
Existing patients undergo periodic lab tests to monitor the efficacy and safety of their treatment.
Self-Scheduling Links
When patients register for our TRT or HRT program and select 'labcorp' as their lab preference, they will schedule a visit. They pay $37 to schedule the visit. The remainder of the fees is charged to our account (not the patient), in most states.
Once the visit has been confirmed, they will receive a text and an email from Labcorp. We also send them a text message from our number with confirmation.
If we need to generate this link for the patient, we can do so by clicking on 'Patient Actions' in Intercom and selecting 'Get the patient's lab scheduling link.'
Once the link is generated, we can text them the link, and they can use the link to schedule the appointment themselves.
If someone wants us to schedule the visit, we can book it for them using the link. It's much faster and more seamless than scheduling directly through Labcorp's site.
Self-Booking Link Issues
The self-booking link may not work for all patients for several reasons. The most common reasons are:
1) There is no Labcorp within 100 miles.
2) The nearby Labcorps does not have availability for at least two weeks.
3) Nearby Labcorp locations do not take appointments.
If the link does not work, a ticket will be created on Intercom to schedule the patient for labs. We will need to use Labcorp's website to see if we can accomodate them. If we can't, we may need to explore Quest or third-party lab options.
â€‹
â€‹
No Labcorps within 100 miles:
-If there are no Labcorps within 100 miles, we can check for Quest locations for the patients. If there are, we can offer them Quest as an option. Please refer to the Quest workflow for more information: https://app.intercom.com/a/apps/g17j5kgu/knowledge-hub/all-content?activeContentId=3151711&activeContentType=internal-article&editorMode=view&search=quest
â€‹
If not, they would need to use third-party labs. We can refer to the Third Party Labs workflow to handle this: https://app.intercom.com/a/apps/g17j5kgu/knowledge-hub/all-content?activeContentId=3228834&activeContentType=internal-article
â€‹
â€‹
Labcorp locations are currently unavailable for two weeks.
-If nearby Labcorp locations do not have availability for two weeks, we can suggest the patient walk to get a sooner appointment. We can send them the macro: LC Booked Out
It appears that nearby Labcorp locations are booked out for the next two weeks. We can check on this in a week to see if there are any openings. Alternatively, you can walk into an office as a walk-in patient and probably get seen. Please note that it may take a while for them to see you if they are full, but most of our patients have good experiences as walk-ins.
â€‹
If you choose to walk, please note you may continue to receive automated lab scheduling messages since the lab visit isn't recorded in our system.
-The patients also have the option of scheduling directly through Labcorp at a later date if they would prefer.
â€‹
â€‹
Nearby Labcorps locations do not take appointments.
It appears that nearby Labcorp locations are not accepting appointments, which is why the booking link may not be working. However, most locations do from our experience, so this is unusual. You do have the option of walking in as a walk-in patient, though, and Labcorp can probably accommodate you. Please note that you may have to wait for a little while for them to take you in, but from our experience, most patients have a good experience as a walk-in patient. If you choose to walk, please note that you may continue to receive automated lab scheduling messages, as the lab visit isn't recorded in our system.
â€‹
Other Locations
-The self-booking link generates locations based on the patient's address. If the patient wants to schedule a LabCorp in another state (i.e, a NY patient wants to schedule in CT), they would need to add a CT address. An alternative zip code is not another to generate this.
-If they have this request, we can send them the macro: LC Self-book different state
Due to the current design of our system, if you wish to schedule an appointment in another state, you will need to provide a full address listed within the zip code you are looking for. It seems a little archaic - and we'll get that fixed when we can. Our team would be happy to assist further if you have any questions.
-If needed, we can generate a fake address/find a Labcorp location for them using Labcorp's site in their preferred zip code. We can plug this into the self-booking link to schedule them.
â€‹
â€‹Scheduling through Labcorp's Website.
-We should almost use the self-booking link for patients. There will be rare cases where it may make sense to use the Labcorp page (i.e the self-booking link is down.)
-If we need to schedule a patient manually through Labcorp's website, please refer to the process below:
Go to Labcorpâ€™s Labs & Appointments page and find the nearest lab location.
Enter the patientâ€™s address or zip code and select â€˜Routine labworkâ€™ as the Reason for your visit.
Click on Make an Appointment on the nearest lab location.
Confirm the patientâ€™s preferred date and time.
Choose â€˜Noâ€™ for Will you be fasting? and set the date on the Appointment Calendar.
Enter the patientâ€™s first and last name, and their sex.
Get the patientâ€™s personal information from their Akute Profile
Enter the patientâ€™s date of birth and address.
Possible Prompt: No Items Found: Manually enter the street address, city, state and zip code
Possible Prompt: We were not able to validate this address. Please review and update, or press Next to continue: Simply click Next once you have entered the address
Always set the Billing option to â€˜I am paying out of pocket todayâ€™ and click Next.
Add the patientâ€™s contact information.
Check the box to agree with receiving a text notification The patient will get confirmation of the appointment after the appointment is booked.
Review & Confirm Appointment for Manual Bookings
New Signups:
Lab Scheduling: Confirmation for New Patients
Here are the appointment details:
Your lab order will be electronically submitted before your appointment and should be in their system by the time of the appointment. You do not need to fast. Our Fountain providers are able to read the numbers under both conditions. If you would prefer to fast, you certainly can but this is not necessary.
Lab orders are submitted under client billing, meaning Labcorp should charge our account in most states. However, if you are in New York, New Jersey, Rhode Island, Massachusetts, or Maryland, you will need to use your insurance.
PLEASE NOTE: Sometimes, the office needs help finding the lab order. If they encounter any issues, please notify us immediately. We'll get that addressed immediately.
We should receive your report within 5-9 days of the blood draw. We'll contact you once we receive the full report. Please reach out if you have any questions!
Lab Scheduling: GLP LC Appt Confirmation
We booked your lab visit for a quick blood draw! There is no need to fast for these labs. Our Fountain providers are able to read the numbers under both conditions. If you would prefer to fast, you certainly can but this is not necessary.
Here are your appointment details:
Your lab order has been electronically submitted to the office prior to your appointment.
Lab orders are submitted under client billing in all states outside of New York, New Jersey, and Rhode Island. If you are in one of these three states you'll need to use your insurance for the lab visit. If you're not in one of those three states they should charge our account (not you.)
If you have any issues while at the office, please text us here immediately and we will assist you. We look forward to working with you!
Monitoring Labs:
Lab Scheduling: Confirmation for Existing Patients
Here are the appointment details:
Your lab order ID is:
The ordering provider is:
Your lab order will be electronically submitted before your appointment and should be in their system by the time of the appointment.
Lab orders are submitted under client billing, meaning Labcorp should charge our account in most states. However, if you are in New York, New Jersey, Rhode Island, Massachusetts, or Maryland, you will need to use your insurance.
PLEASE NOTE: Sometimes, the office needs help finding the lab order. If they encounter any issues, please notify us immediately. We'll get that addressed immediately.
We should receive your report within 5-9 days of the blood draw. We'll contact you once we receive the full report. Please reach out if you have any questions!
Confirmation Emails and Texts
When patient appointments are booked, they receive a text and email with appointment confirmation details.
They can click on the link in the email to be directed to a page with a QR code, which they can use to check in for their appointment.
We cannot send this information to patients ourselves, as it comes directly from Labcorp.
Patients typically receive an automated text reminder 24 hours before their Labcorp appointment.
We cannot send this info out to patients since Labcorp is a third party and we can not send these messages out on their behalf.
Common Questions:
Can you send me the QR code from the appointment confirmation?
LC: Can you send me a QR code?
Labcorp should have sent out an email with a QR code. If you don't see an email from Labcorp please check your spam folder.
Can you send me the confirmation email/text Labcorp sent me?
LC: Labcorp Confirmation Email
Labcorp should have sent out an email with a QR code. We're unable to send out the code ourselves since Labcorp sends out the text themselves. If you don't see the email from Labcorp please check your spam folder.
Lab Order Requests
If a patient needs a new lab order, create a RN ticket indicating what type of lab order the patient needs (new patient labs or follow up patient labs.)
Indicate what type of lab order the patient needs.
â€‹
â€‹The RN will create the lab order, leave a note indicating the lab order has been written, and reassign the ticket back to CS..
CS should provide the patient with their lab Order ID and resolve the ticket once it's reassigned to CS.
â€‹
Weekend Availability
If we are unable to schedule an appointment at the patientâ€™s preferred times, we can check if there is a Labcorp location available on weekends. Some locations are open on Saturdays between 7 and 11AM, although this may vary depending on the location. Labcorp does not operate on Sundays.
When searching using the patient's zip code, you can filter the list by selecting the'Open on weekends' option.
Even if a Labcorp location is farther away, the patient may be willing to go if it has weekend availability.
Geographic Issues
Patients may express inconvenience or frustration about Labcorp locations for various reasons, including but not limited to:
Labcorp has been booked out or fully booked.
The nearest Labcorp is located out of town and will require a long drive.
The nearest Labcorp location has permanently closed.
There is no nearby Labcorp location nearby.
Demonstrate empathy towards the patient's situation and help them find the suitable option to fulfill their blood work requirement.
If Labcorp is Booked Out
In some states, Labcorp can be quite booked out, which causes patients to become upset. It is both in the patient's best interest and our best interest to get these patients in sooner so are more engaged in the program. If a patient indicates they want a sooner appointment we should do the following:
See if there are any locations further away that can take the appointment in sooner.
Offer them the option of walking into the office
See if any Quest offices near the patient can take the patient sooner.
Common Questions
Why are visits so booked out?
LC: Visits are Booked Out - check other locations.
We can't comment on any specific location - but there is high demand in some areas which is increasing wait times for visits at Labcorp locations. We're happy to check other locations, or look for nearby Quest offices.
LC: Visits are Booked Out - walk In
We've checked nearby Labcorp and Quest locations, but they are all booked out. If you'd like to get in sooner you can walk into Labcorp. If you choose to do this, you may have to wait at the office for some time before they take you in. Our providers will submit the lab order electronically prior to your visit. Please note that you do not need to fast for your visit. If you encounter any issues at the office, please text us ASAP so we can assist you.
Why can't you get me in sooner?
LC: Why can't I get into Labcorp sooner?
Labcorp is a third party - we do not have any control of their available time slots. We share your frustration that they're booked out - but we're happy to look for alternative locations that can get you in sooner. We can also search for nearby Quest offices that may be able to take you in sooner.
If I walk into Labcorp will I have to wait longer since they are so backed out?
Labs: Long Wait Times for Walk-In
It's certainly possible that you may have a wait time if you walk in to Labcorp, given that the location is currently booked out.
If Labcorp is Closed
Sometimes patients arrive at Labcorp only to find the office is closed. Labcorp is supposed to send out an email to patients indicating that the location is closed prior to their appointment, but they don't always do this. The patient may be a tad irate.
LC: Labcorp is Closed
Labcorp allowed us to book at that location, and gave us no indication of any sudden closures. They are supposed to send patients an email when this happens - but it appears they failed to do so. We can reschedule you at another location. What times/dates would work for you?
If a Patient Indicates They Cannot Find the Office
Sometimes patients indicate they cannot find the Labcorp location that we scheduled them at. We don't have any additional information to specifically help them locate the office beyond what we could find on google maps.
If they're simply unable to find the Labcorp office, and reschedule them at another location, please send the snippet:
LC: Hard to Find Labcorp Location
Hi, we booked the appointment at: but we don't have any additional information since all we do is submit the order, and schedule the appointment through their website. If you're unable to find it please let us know and we can try to schedule you at another location.
Specific Towns or Areas
Sometimes, patients indicate that they'd like us to look for specific areas to schedule them. You can typically type in the location of the area on Labcorp's website and it should come up.
If it doesn't come up, you can send the macro: LC: Unable to Find Specific Area Provided by the Patient
LC: Unable to Find Specific Area Provided by the Patient
We put the location you provided into Labcorp's website, but it didn't come up. Could you please provide the zip code of that area? Thank you.
Zip Code Issues
Occasionally, Labcorp's site auto-fills the last zip code used.
If this happens, please send the patient the macro: LC: Wrong Zip Code and add the correct zip code.
If the issue continues, please ask the patient for other zip codes near them.
Labcorp's site may not be able to find the zip code the patient provided.
Take Note: Please note that we should be using the buttons on Intercom to schedule patients, which should reduce the likelihood of this occurring.
LC: Wrong Zip Code
LabCorp's site appears to have loaded your zip code incorrectly. Here's the nearest location:
Website Issues
We may be unable to book appointments with Labcorp due to technical issues with their site.
If we cannot book the appointment since their webpage does not work or there is some other error, please send the patient the macro: LC: Tech Issues
Please create a ticket on Intercom and snooze it for one hour.
Once the ticket reopens, please see if you can schedule the patient. If you can, schedule them.
If not, snooze the ticket for another hour.
Please do not snooze the conversation since the patient may respond to the message and break the snooze.
LC: Tech Issues
Hey - weâ€™re currently experiencing technical issues with the LabCorp website. Weâ€™ll schedule you once the website loads correctly. Thanks for your patience.
Alternative Lab Testing
Getlabs
Check the patientâ€™s Akute profile to determine if they are eligible to receive at-home labs through Getlabs.
Take Note: Note that Getlabs is NOT applicable for GLP Patients.
Quest
If a patient prefers to visit another local testing center or is not eligible for Getlabs, we can schedule them with Quest. We can cover the cost of bloodwork at Quest for TRT patients. We cannot cover the costs for HRT patients. For more information, please refer to the Quest Workflow.
Out-of-State Labcorp Visits
If neither Getlabs, Labcorp, nor Quest offices are available near the patient's location, please ask them the following:
LC - Traveling
Iâ€™m sorry that we have been unable to find a convenient location for you. Are you traveling anytime soon? Itâ€™s possible we can schedule you at an office near your destination.
If yes, confirm the zip code and schedule a Labcorp appointment there.
Take Note: We can only schedule Labcorp appointments in the United States. We cannot schedule the patient if they are abroad.
TRT/HRT: Other Providers
If a patient prefers to have lab work done at a local provider's office or is not near a Labcorp or Quest location, our medical team can submit an order for use at an alternative provider.
Take Note: Please note that any fees associated with using a third-party provider will not be covered.
Process for Third-Party Labwork Requests
If the patient is interested in/willing to have their lab work done at a third-party office, please create an 'RN Ticket' and use the â€œThird-party lab orderâ€ option.
The ticket will automatically be assigned to the providers if this option is selected.

GLP: Other Providers
In situations where Labcorp and Quest are unavailable for the patient, we can also suggest that they consider visiting a local provider. It would be beneficial to offer this alternative option to them as well. Please create a ticket on Intercom requesting a new lab order.
Labs: Third-party labs option
We do have another option for bloodwork. We can put in an order for labs and send it to you or fax over to an alternative testing location. Please note that we would be unable the labs fees if you chose to use this option. If you have insurance it's probable (although not guaranteed), that it would be covered by an alternative provider.

Walk-Ins
-Most Labcorp locations allow patients to walk in without needing an appointment. If a patient wants to go in sooner than the next available date or indicates they have an unpredictable schedule, we should tell them to walk in.
-This option is not available through the self-booking link at this time. We do intend to add it to the self-scheduling link in the future.
LC: Walk-In
Yes, you can walk into most locations without an appointment. However, certain locations may not accept walk-ins, so we recommend checking ahead of time.

Common Questions
How long will I need to wait?
LC: How long will I need to wait at Labcorp?
They should hopefully take you in shortly after you arrive, but wait times will vary depending on the location.
Do I need to let Labcorp know if I walk in?
LC: Do I need to let them know if I walk in?
You do not need to let Labcorp know if you walk in. The lab order should be in their system, and they should be able to take you in based on that. If there are any issues, please text us asap.
Calling Labcorp
If the results of a patient's lab visit are not received within a week, there was likely an issue. In such cases, it is recommended that you contact Labcorp to ask about the status update. Before calling Labcorp, be ready with the Labcorp client billing number which can be found in any lab order.
Fax will not automatically sync with the patient's Akute profile. We need to add the document to the patient's profile.
To add the fax to the patient's profile:
Open the task and check the patient's name and DOB in the document.
Type the patient's name under Patient.
Click Save.
Bills
Overturning The Bills
Patients should not be billed for bloodwork we order unless they are in New York, New Jersey, Rhode Island, Massachusetts, or Maryland. Those states have special rules for in-person lab visits that require patients to use their insurance for the visit. This process does not apply to those patients.
Outside of the states above, orders are submitted under client billing - meaning they should charge their our account, not the patient.
Occasionally, these patients do get charged due to confusion at the office or due to them providing their insurance card to the office. This will cause LabCorp to bill to their insurance rather than billing to the client (us.)
If a patient indicates they received a bill and is NOT in New York, New Jersey, Rhode Island, Massachusetts, or Maryland, please send them the macro: LC: Step 1 of Bill Process - Requesting bill to be sent over to us
Once the patient sends over the bill, please check to see if it was ordered by us (Fountain). You should be able to see the account name and the ordering provider listed on the bill.
If the patient received a bill for the tests we ordered:
If it's clear that the patient received a bill for the tests we ordered we'll need to email our Labcorp representative. Please send them the macro: LC: Bill Transfer
Please email Chelsie Tripp (chelsie.tripp1@labcorp.com), our Labcorp rep, and send the macro: LC: Emailing Labcorp for a Bill
If the patient received a bill for tests that Fountain did not order:
Sometimes, patients do receive bills from Labcorp due to confusion at the office. We can overturn these bills and transfer them over to our account. If a patient indicates they received a bill from Labcorp, please send them the macro: LC: Bill - Alternative Provider. Once the patient responds, send the macro: LC: Bill - Alternative Provider Response
LC: Step 1 of Bill Process - Requesting bill to be sent over to us
Got it. Can you please send over a screenshot of the bill or invoice number? Our team would be happy to look into it. Lab orders are submitted under client billing in all states other than New York, New Jersey, Rhode Island, Massachusetts and Maryland, meaning they should have charged our account. It's certainly possible they made a mistake - which we should be able to address.
LC: Bill Transfer
Thanks for sending us over. Labcorp should have billed our account, not you. This may have been due to an error by someone at the office. We'll email our Labcorp rep now. Please know that it can take between 30-90 days for the bill to be transferred over successfully to our account. During this time you may receive collection notices from Labcorp. Please disregard them. You will receive a notice from Labcorp once the bill has been transferred over.
LC: Emailing Labcorp for a Bill
Hi - this is ___ from Fountain's Customer Service Team. [Patient Name] received a bill from Labcorp. The PSC was: and the date of the appointment was: Please transfer this bill over to Fountain's Account (Account Number 09529930) Thank you very much!
LC: Bill - Alternative Provider
It looks like the tests were ordered by: they are not associated with our practice. Are you familiar with a provider with the name of the office or provider who wrote ordered the tests listed on the bill?
LC: Bill - Alternative Provider Response
Got it. I'd suggest reaching out to Labcorp at: 800-845-6167 or the practice itself if you have any questions regarding this bill. Have a great day.
Lab Charge Disputes
If patients report that Labcorp is attempting to charge them
LC: Client Billing Issue
Hello,ï»¿Labcorp should have charged our account. The order is submitted under 'client billing', in your state, which means they are supposed to bill us (the client.) This was a mistake on their end. We'll be able to transfer it over to our account -- we just need a screenshot of the bill. Thank you!
If the patient becomes irate at any point and requests a refund, process the refund and send the following message:
Sincerest apologies for this inconvenience. This is usually a seamless process, but they are having issues at the office. We'll refund your assessment fee.
Take Note: Please note the refund will take 3-5 business days to process. We can still reschedule your lab appointment at a different location at no additional cost if you're still interested.
If Labcorp refuses to process the order under client billing, send the following message:
The order has been submitted under 'client billing,' which means Labcorp is supposed to bill us (the client). If Labcorp is unable to process your order under our client billing, you will receive an invoice directly from them. Please send it to us, and we will transfer it to our account. We apologize for any inconvenience caused.
Lab Order Issues
All lab orders should be electronically submitted prior to the patient's appointment.
All new TRT and HRT patients have lab orders created for them after completing the evaluation.
GLP orders are still manually submitted by the medical team.
Follow-up lab orders are submitted by either the NPs, RNs, or MAs after a patient's consultation. Occasionally these are not submitted and can cause issues.
When a patient indicates LabCorp cannot find the order
If a patient indicates they're at Labcorp please let them know that we're checking on this right now, and will get back to them asap.
We need to prioritize communication with these patients above others since they are already a bit annoyed.
Please visit HealthGorilla to check if there is a valid lab order for the patient. While most orders sync to Akute, sometimes they donâ€™t, or the last order may have already been used, which wonâ€™t be visible on Akute.
If you see that the last order was 'sent', within the last six months the lab order should be valid.
If the lab order is older than six months we will need a new lab order sent out for the patient.
If it's 'completed', or a 'draft', we'll need a new lab order to be submitted for the patient.
If the order is valid please provide the patient with the ordering provider's name and the lab order ID.
You can send this out using the macro: Labs: LC/Quest Unable to Find Patient's Order
If the order is not valid please send the macro: Labs: Invalid Order Info to the patient. Please send a WhatsApp message to the shared group.
Please indicate that the patient is at LC/Quest and we need a new lab order to be submitted for the patient.
Once the order is submitted, someone from our medical team will let us know.
We should send the patient the macro: Labs: If patient's lab order is in

Incomplete Lab Results
Sometimes Labcorp will have an issue with the specimen collected from patients, and they won't be able to process some lab results.
When this happens, we'll first need to call Labcorp to confirm the status of the lab results that have not been processed and ask if the patient will need to go back in for a redraw.
If lab results have been processed successfully
Provide the Labcorp representative with our fax number: 202-998-4218, and ask for it to be faxed to us
Sync the fax with the patientâ€™s Akute profile when it comes in
From the Akute Documents, delete the results that include the incomplete results if they were uploaded previously
If the patient needs to go back in for a redraw due to insufficient specimen collected
Confirm with Labcorp if the same lab order can be used for redraw, or if we need to submit a new lab order.
Request for a new lab order if needed:
Refer to TRT Requesting a new lab order
Refer to GLP Requesting a new lab order
Reach out to the patient and advise that a redraw is needed and schedule a new lab appointment for them (Refer to: Labcorp Scheduling)
Incomplete Lab Results: Patient Communication
If the patient needs a redraw due to an issue with the sample we should send them the macro:
LC: Blood Test Redraw
Hey, this is: from Fountain. We contacted Labcorp and asked for an update on your results. They indicated there was an issue with the sample which caused the results not be processed correctly. This is frustrating - and they did not communicate with us in any capacity regarding this issue. We will need to schedule you for a new blood draw. What times/dates would work for you?
Please make sure that a new valid lab order is available for the patient (if needed).
If the patient is upset and requests a refund, please refund them regardless of whether we paid for their original blood draw.
Incorrect Lab Order Details
Patient Name or Date of Birth Mismatch
If the provider has submitted a new lab order for a patient and cannot find the order, it is possible that we have the wrong date of birth (DOB) or name on file for the patient. We need to confirm with the patient if the name and DOB we have on file is correct. If there is a mismatch, they may not be able to locate the order.
Account Number Not Found
If the client number cannot be found:
Provide our client billing number listed on the order: 09529930
If they cannot enter the order into their system:
Inform the patient that this is the information we have always used. Apologize for the inconvenience caused by Labcorp's technical problems. Request our medical team to send another lab order electronically, and hopefully, the account number will be displayed correctly.
Send a message to the medical team on WhatsApp and ask them to send an order for the patient. Explain that Labcorp is unable to process the current order due to technical issues on their end.
Incorrect Lab Order Used
Sometimes results are delayed due Labcorp mixing up patient lab orders. They sometimes use a lab order for the wrong patient, or perform bloodwork using another order on file for the patient.
If a patient confirms that they went to their visit but results haven't come back after 5-7 days, please call Labcorp. If Labcorp indicates that they used the wrong order so we haven't received the results, send the macro:
LC: Labcorp Sent Wrong Lab Order
Hello - we reached out to Labcorp, and the representative we spoke with indicated the wrong lab order was used for the test. They used a lab order that was submitted by: We will need to reschedule your visit due to this. What times/dates would work for you?

Frequently Asked Questions (FAQs)
If a patient indicates that they couldn't get the bloodwork done because of an outstanding balance at Labcorp.
Labs: Unable to get the bloodwork done due to an outstanding balance at Labcorp
Understood. We've asked LabCorp about this situation before, and they've indicated that they cannot perform the bloodwork we ordered for you until the past-due balance is addressed. Please let us know when it is, and we'll reschedule your appointment.
Can you cancel my lab visit?
LC: Can you cancel my lab visit?
We can't cancel the lab visit after we book the visit and leave their page - but you can simply not show up to the visit. You may receive a reminder text/email from Labcorp which you can disregard.
Can they do the lab tests my provider ordered in addition to the ones you ordered?
LC: Can they do the tests my doctor ordered as well?
Labcorp is typically able to use multiple different lab orders at the same appointment. If you encounter any issues for whatever reason please let us know.
Do you partner with other lab services?
Labs: Do you partner with other labs?
Yes! We partner with Quest Diagnostics and cover the cost of labs for patients in our TRT program outside of New York, New Jersey, Rhode Island, Massachusetts, and Maryland. Patients in our HRT program will need to use their insurance to cover the cost of the blood work. We're happy to look for appointments at Quest if you'd prefer to go there.
Where are you located?
General CS: Where are you located?
Hello - thanks for reaching out. Fountain Vitality is a telemedicine company, we have team members located throughout the US and abroad.
Can I bring my kids with me to the lab visit?
LC: Can I bring my kids with me to the lab visit?
Labcorp has indicated that children must always be supervised during the lab collection.
Can I bring my dog with me to the visit?
LC: Can I bring an animal with me to my visit?
Labcorp has indicated that unless you have a trained service animal, they do not allow pets to accompany you during visits.
Can my friend go to Labcorp with me?
LC: Can my friend go to Labcorp with me?
Generally, someone above the age of 18 can accompany you to Labcorp and wait in the waiting room, but they are not permitted to enter the testing facilities with you.
Does Labcorp have a dress code for patients?
LC: Do they have a dress code for patients?
Labcorp does not have a dress code for patients.

Messages from Unregistered Leads
Messages from Unregistered Leads
Overview
This document covers common questions from unregistered patients. They are contacting us through multiple different channels, including, but not limited to, Intercom SMS, Phone, Email, and Facebook.
Communication
When people message us who haven't registered, we do not always see patient names, DOBs, or any other identifying information.
We see their phone numbers.
We cannot search for these patients on our platforms since they aren't registered.
Some of these patients may respond with messages like 'okay', which does not require action. You can close the conversation if you encounter a message like this. They are responding to abandoned cart emails in most cases, which do not require any action from us.
Common Questions
These potential patients often have many repeated questions. Please refer to the common questions in this section to determine the best responses.
Phone Calls and Communication
Can I call you?
General CS: Can I call you?
Sure. Please feel free to call us at 1213-237-1454.
What is your contact number?
Contact Numbers
You can text us at: (833) 967-3100â€‹ï»¿ï»¿
You can call us at: 1213 237 1454
Can you text my friend the evaluation link?
Texting Unregistered Contacts
Due to our backend customer service platform, we cannot text unregistered contacts proactively. If they want to reach out and text us here, they certainly can, or they can call us at 833-967-3100. Thank you so much!
Do I have to have a valid phone number to register?
General CS: Valid phone number
Due to our backend structure, you will need a valid US phone number to register. You would need the phone number to receive texts.
If an unregistered contact indicated that they had already completed the assessment
Once they provide the email, please go to Stripe and search to see if there is a patient with their email.
If the patient comes up, please use the information on Stripe to find their Akute profile and follow up with them accordingly.
If the patient does not come up, please send the following message:

If they send the screenshot over and it's a different service, please use the below macro:
Unknown SMS: Registered with a different service
Thanks for sending that over. It looks like you registered with: and not with Fountain. Wishing you the best in your wellness journey!

-If the sender sends us a message that makes it looks like they are an active patient (i.e I am having side effects, I am an active patient, etc), please send them the macro: Update Info
We'd be happy to help out with this. It looks like you may be contacting through another number/email that we do not have on file for you. Can you please confirm your full name, your date of birth, your phone number, and your email?

HRT: What labs do you order?

What are the costs?
Fountain Pricing

Can I receive free samples?
General CS: Can I receive a free sample?
We are not legally allowed to provide samples, especially for controlled substances.
What is the maximum dose you can prescribe?
General CS: Highest Dose
Our medical team has indicated the following:
Fountain TRT has a maximum dosage of 200 mg/week, and a dose up to 200mg/week would be included in the cost. Your provider determines your specific dose at the time of consultation, considering your symptoms, medical history, and lab results. Please let us know if you have any further questions.

Assessment Link

Is lab work covered under the assessment fee?
Billing: Assessment Fee Coverage
The assessment fee covers the cost of labs, consultation, and support in most states. Please note that New York, New Jersey, and Maryland have unique rules for in-person lab visits, which require patients to use their insurance for in-person lab visits.
Can I use my own labs?
Labs: If the patient already has labs
We ask that all patients use the lab set we ordered. This ensures that we have all the updated values for all the specific tests that we order.
Can I get labs done through you before I register?

Due to the way our backend is set up our medical team cannot write orders for patients who have not registered.
Treatment Timeline
Some unregistered leads ask how long it will take them to get the medication after registering. It depends on lab availability and how far out visits are in their state.
If they ask, we can send them the below macro:
General CS: Timeline to start treatment
The timeline to start treatment is not fixed since it depends on how soon you get your labs, see a provider, and start your subscription, but typically the timeline is within 2-3 weeks of registering.
The lead may want us to check to see nearby lab locations or how booked out visits are in their state.
Assessment-related Inquiries (macros)
General CS: TRT Discounted Assessment Link
Hi, you can complete the assessment for just $5 using this link: trytrt.joinftn.com. We look forward to working with you.
General CS: TRT Registration Link
Sure! Hereâ€™s the link: https://fountaintrt.com/
Billing: Assessment Fee Coverage
The assessment fee covers the cost of labs, consultation, and support in most states. Please note that New York, New Jersey, and Maryland have unique rules for in-person lab visits, which require patients to use their insurance for in-person lab visits.
Unregistered Patient: How do I see my assessment results?
The assessment determines whether you qualify for a Personalized Evaluation in your state. Once you sign up, you will have access to lab testing and a provider video visit to discuss your results and determine whether TRT treatment is appropriate for you.
General CS: Do you treat women?
We recently launched our HRT program available exclusively for women! You can find more info here: https://fountain.net/hrt
Please let us know if you have any questions.
Do you participate in insurance?
Billing: Insurance
Hello - Fountain does not participate with insurance. We certainly understand the reasons to try to find an insurance-based program. However, it doesn't work for Fountain's model for a few reasons. T therapy is frequently not well covered or covered only after a large deductible is paid each year. Treatment decisions are often directed by insurance companies and instead of the shared decisions of patients and providers.
Fountain's billing model is based on an all-inclusive fee that recurs periodically and covers all aspects of service (medication, supplies, shipping costs, taxes, provider visits, labs, and 24/7 direct access to providers and support team via our text messages platform). We are able to give more personalized attention and time to fewer individual patients.
Video Visits
How soon can I see a provider?
VV: How soon can I see a provider?
It would depend on the particular state you are located in (our providers have different state licenses), but generally, you would be able to see a provider within 5-14 days.
Iâ€™m in X - state how soon could I see a provider?
You should check the state-based scheduling link and see when the next available appointment is.
VV: Next visit available in X state
I just checked our calendar, and it looks like the next available point in your state would be:
Do you do in-person appointments?
Unknown SMS: Do you do in-person appointments?
At this time all of our appointments are done virtually from the comfort of your home/office.
Treatment Eligibility
What are the reference ranges for testosterone?
Medical: Testosterone Reference Ranges
Hello, reference ranges for total testosterone levels can vary from lab to lab. Our providers interpret results on a case-by-case basis as a certain testosterone level for one person can be adequate while the same level for another person can cause significant symptoms.
They also take into account each patient's 'free' testosterone level.
My T level is X, do I qualify for treatment?
General CS: Do I qualify for TRT treatment?
I'm not a medical provider so I can't directly answer that. But in general, our providers prescribe based on lots of different variables such as your symptoms, and not just whether T is at a specific number.
The reason for this is that every man responds to different testosterone levels differently â€” there's not necessarily one set number every man should be at.
I'm already on TRT. Will I have to stop my current regimen?
Unregistered Patient: Already on TRT
Hi there, our providers are very experienced with helping guys already on TRT. That's why we do a Personalized TRT Evaluation first, so you can speak to a FountainTRT provider about your specific needs.
To get started, you just need to sign up through our online assessment. Here's a link to have your personalized evaluation for just $5: https://trytrt.joinftn.com/
What pharmacies do you use?
General CS: What pharmacies do you use?
Most prescriptions are filed through one of our partner pharmacies, Belmar, the Pharmacy Hub, or Curexa.

We cannot split payments due to the way our subscription system works.

We do not have a contract. You can reach out at any point before your next scheduled subscription charge to cancel the service.

Medications Prescribed (macros)
General CS: What type of testosterone injections do you prescribe?
Our providers prescribe Testosterone Cypionate.
General CS: Do you prescribe an oral option?
Our providers can prescribe Enclomiphene.
General CS: Do you prescribe B12 shots?
Our providers do not currently prescribe B12 injections.
General CS: Do you prescribe cream/oral medication?
Our medical team prescribes oral treatment or offers daily cream-based, trans-labial testosterone (by itself or with estrogen), which allows for easy dose adjustments and even, consistent benefits. Please let us know if you have any other questions.
General CS: Do you prescribe desiccated thyroid medication?
Yes! Our providers can prescribe that for you if you are on an active HRT subscription. The plan would be billed at $75 every 12 weeks.
General CS: Do you prescribe ED meds?
Yes, our providers can prescribe either sildenafil or tadalafil.
General CS: Do you prescribe HGH?
Our providers do not currently prescribe HGH.
General CS: Do you prescribe injections as well as cream?
Yes, our medical team can prescribe either cream or injections.
General CS: Do you prescribe patches or pellets?
Our providers do not currently prescribe patches or pellets. We prescribe Enclomiphene, TRT Cream, and TRT Injections.
General CS: Do you prescribe peptides?
Our providers do not prescribe peptides at this time.
General CS: Do you prescribe spironolactone?
Yes! Our providers can prescribe that for you if you are on an active HRT subscription. The plan would be billed at $25 every 12 weeks.
General CS: Do you prescribe supplements?
Our providers do not prescribe supplements at this time.
General CS: Do you prescribe Testosterone Enanthate?
Our providers do not prescribe testosterone enanthate at this time.
General CS: Do you prescribe HRT injections?
Our medical team does not prescribe injections for patients in our HRT program at this time. Our medical team prescribes oral treatment or offers daily cream-based, trans-labial testosterone.
General CS: What medications are prescribed in the TRT plan?
The cost of the plan is for either TRT cream, Enclomphine, or Injections. The plan is billed upfront and recurs on a subscription cycle.
General CS: What medications are prescribed in the GLP plan?
Our providers prescribe semaglutide and sublingual semaglutide.
Medical: Is the T Cyp you prescribe bioidentical?
Our partner pharmacy (Curexa), has indicated the following: The chemical formula for testosterone is C19H28O2 whereas the chemical formula for testosterone cypionate is C27H40O3. Testosterone cypionate is a prodrug that needs to be broken down in your body to be absorbed effectively.
Once it IS broken down, it is bioidentical to testosterone. But not before.
Medical: What kind of testosterone cream do you prescribe?
We typically prescribe testosterone cream that our partner pharmacy compounds, so there is no brand name. The cream is designed to give the highest concentration of testosterone in a consistency that can be easily applied and absorbed.
It dries quickly, has no odor, and looks and feels like moisturizer lotion. Each UnoDose click of the cream contains 50 mg of testosterone.
Medical: What kind of needles are prescribed?
The needles are: 27G 1/2" 1 cc syringes.

Our medical team has indicated that if you are menstruating the day of your labwork, there shouldn't be any issues. Our providers can read the labs and interpret them based on where you are in your cycle at the time the labs are drawn.

Our medical team does not prescribe injections for patients in our HRT program at this time. Our medical team prescribes oral treatment or offers daily cream-based, trans-labial testosterone.

Fountain is over 3 years old and we have over 4000 active members. All of our providers are U.S-licensed. Our program is LegitScript certified, and our partner pharmacies are US-licensed.
For more info, you can check us out at Fountain.net

New RXs and Refills
New RXs and Refills
Overview
Starting a subscription, all patients have new RXs sent to our partner
pharmacy. Refills are processed based on the medication type and the supply
sent to the patient.
Our system for handling refills and new RXs has changed over time as we've
worked to determine the best one.
New RXs
New RXs are processed based on the billing ticket the provider sends over after the video visit (see below sample).
Once the patient has been charged, the ticket is assigned to the MAs. They check the PMP (Prescription Monitoring Program) and add the pharmacy, med codes, and the RX data line to the ticket.
Once this has been added and the PMP is transparent (they aren't receiving medication through another provider), the ticket is reassigned to the provider to submit the RX.
Refills and Expired RXs
Our system generates a ticket 14 days before a patient is due for a controlled substance refill.
This ticket is routed to the MAs by default to see if they are eligible for a refill (meaning they had a video visit with a provider recently). If they have, they add the relevant information to the ticket and reassign the ticket to the provider.
If they are not eligible for a new RX, we send them a scheduling link for a video visit with a provider.
This is usually sent by our system, regardless of 30 days before they're due for their refill, but this is a backup system to see if they actually attended the visit.
These tickets are generated 7 days before the patient is due for a refill for a non-controlled substance since they don't need a new visit. They are routed to the MAs and then to the provider who submits the RX.
Other
This system only works for patients who:
Have active subscriptions, and
Are not past due on payments.
Patients who have orders sent to local pharmacies are not handled through this system. Those patients will need to contact us directly to process refills since it's outside our system.
â€‹

Labs
New Sign Ups and Monitoring Labs Workflow
New Sign Ups and Monitoring Labs Workflow
Overview
Our system for handling lab visits has changed dramatically over the last few months. Due to this, the monitoring labs workflow and the new signups workflow are heavily outdated.
This document covers the recent changes and addresses the most common questions. It will functionally replace the new sign-ups and monitoring lab workflows.
â€‹
Process Flow for New Signups
When new users register, they have several different options for labs available to them.
They self-schedule their lab visits through our website.
They select Skip this for now, and get their labs scheduled after they register.
It is possible that a patient selects some of the steps but does not fill them all out.
This generates a ticket called Schedule Labcorp Labs, for these patients. We should try manually scheduling these patients using the unique link on Intercom. If that doesn't work, we will need to schedule these patients directly on LabCorp's site.
They select Getlabs, and get their labs scheduled after they register.
We are actively working on automating the Getlabs process so patients can self-schedule.
We have also discussed removing the Skip this for now option from the flow and requiring patients to provide their preferred times/dates.
Since 5/15, all follow-ups for new registered patients have been handled through our system. Some backdated new sign-up tickets from patients who registered before this date may still exist, but these tickets should not be created for new patients.
Process Flow for Monitoring Labs
TRT patients are due for monitoring labs approximately six weeks after receiving their first shipment. HRT patients are due for monitoring labs around 8 weeks after they receive their first shipment.
Each patient's lab date should appear on the sidebar on Intercom if they meet the following criteria:
Have an active TRT/HRT subscription. GLP patients do not have this date, nor do patients on ED only plans.
Is not past due on their subscription. If they are past due, this date does not appear.
On the Next labs contact date, the system will send the patient a message to self-schedule their labs.
In most cases, the patient will self-schedule, and the system will check to see if they got their results back within 5 days of the visit.
If patients have not received their results back within 5 days of the scheduled visit, the system will ask if they attended. In the vast majority of cases, patients have scheduled but simply did not attend these visits. They will use the self-scheduling link to reschedule their appointment.
The system will send a message once the lab work has synced with our system, indicating it's time to schedule their visit. In the vast majority of cases, the system will notify the patient within 24 hours of the results coming back. In some cases, it may take up to 48.
Self-Scheduling Links and Getlabs
The self-scheduling for new signups and monitoring labs patients is the same. We can load it on our end by clicking under 'patient actions' in the sidebar on Intercom.
The actual process of self-scheduling is very similar to Labcorp's website - but it's faster and more efficient.
Patients (or us in some cases) provide their address, select Getlabs or Labcorp, provide the preferred times/dates for the lab visit, select the location, and schedule.
The patient should receive an automated text and email from Labcorp confirming the lab visit. We will also text them a confirmation within 20 minutes of scheduling the lab visit.
Self-scheduling and Tech Issues
When a patient selects Getlabs
When a patient skips the Self-Scheduling option
Walk-Ins
Our current lab scheduling system does not account for walk-ins. That being said, patients still can walk into Labcorp if they want to.
â€‹
Please send them the Labs: LC Walk In macro:
Yes, you can walk into most locations without an appointment. However, certain locations may not accept walk-ins, so we recommend checking ahead of time. â€‹ï»¿ï»¿
Please note that you may receive automated follow-up messages regarding your lab visit that do not consider that you are walking in for your visit.
If you receive these, please disregard them if you intended your visit. â€‹ï»¿ï»¿If the lab has any issues finding the order, please text us ASAP, and we'll sort it out.
They will receive automated follow-up messages indicating they still need to schedule their Labcorp visit. We are working on adding a walk-in option to the self-scheduling process.
Objections to Self-Scheduling
Some patients who have been in our care for a while may object to self-scheduling since they are very used to us booking the visits for them.
â€‹
Objection: Why don't you book the labs anymore?
If a patient asks why we are asking patients to self-schedule, we can send them the Labs: Objections to self-scheduling macro:
We'd be happy to book the appointment for you if you prefer. The reason behind this change is that many patients found it more efficient to self-schedule their visits and found the back-and-forth regarding the lab visit sometimes disorganized. We still have that option available for any patients who would prefer us to schedule the visit for them, though.
Objection: Getlabs Costs
We previously covered the cost of Getlabs for existing patients and only charged new patients $99 for the service. Some patients are used to us covering this service and may object. If they do, please send them the Labs: Objections to Getlabs Costs macro:
Our costs have gone up across the board, and we're trying to keep the membership rate the same for as long as possible for patients in our care. We covered the costs of the at-home phlebotomy as long as we could, but unfortunately, we had to make this change recently.
If A New Sign-up Wants to Use Getlabs After Registering
New sign-ups are typically charged a $99 fee if they choose to use Getlabs in addition to the standard evaluation fee.
If a new sign-up indicates that they'd prefer to switch to Getlabs and Getlabs covers their area, we can set this up for them.
Check first to see if they are in an area that Getlabs services.
Check the Getlabs Eligible status on the patientâ€™s Akute profile
If the patient is not in the area that GetLabs services, please send them the Getlabs: No coverage macro:
We partner with a mobile lab company that send specialists for at-home blood draws. Unfortunately, they don't yet cover every area of the country, including your zip code.
If they are in the area, please send the Getlabs: One Time Charge macro:
Sure, we're happy to set that up for you. Please note that there is an additional fee for the home blood draw option. We would need to run a one-time payment of $99 to the card on file ending with: Please let us know if that works for you.
Once the patient confirms, go to their Stripe profile and select Create payment. Run a $99 charge to their card.
If the payment goes through, schedule them through Getlabs.
If the payment does not go through, send them the Billing: New Signup charge for Getlabs does not go through macro:
It looks like the transaction had declined when processing the Getlabs fee. Is the payment method ending in: the intended payment method? If needed, you can change your payment method using this link:
Please note that the link will expire within 48 hours for security reasons.
Multiple Patients Registering with One Profile
Occasionally (albeit rarely), patients register for multiple programs with one user profile. This is usually HRT and TRT. Sometimes, couples would like to register for the programs together.
Unlike most services where one profile works seamlessly across multiple programs, we may encounter issues due to several factors, including but not limited to:
HIPAA - they would need to share a phone number, which is a violation.
Lab results will not sync correctly.
Prescriptions and orders will not go through properly since they can only be matched to one patient.
We'll likely discover this based on conversations with the patient, which indicate that they registered for multiple people.
We may also be able to see this if we know that they registered for multiple programs that don't make sense (e.g., TRT and HRT). A man cannot register for our HRT program, and a woman cannot register for our TRT program since the treatment protocols are distinct.
If we notice situations where a single user profile has been used to register for multiple programs or on behalf of multiple people, we should attempt to call the patient and explain that our system will run into issues if they register on behalf of two people.
This will cause prescriptions to not be adequately processed and shipments to not go out as intended. We can refund the additional assessment fee they paid, but the person they registered on behalf of will need to complete the relevant assessment.
If they do not want a call/are unavailable, we can send them the General CS: Patient registered on behalf of another macro:
No problem. I just wanted to let you know that you registered on behalf of multiple people for our programs. We're so glad to hear that you know people interested in our programs - but due to how our backend works, they would need to register themselves. They will not have a unique chart in our system, which means that prescriptions cannot be appropriately written or filled. We can refund the additional assessment fees you paid, but they'll need to complete the assessment for the programs they're interested in.
Patients Who Register for Multiple Programs
Patients can register for multiple programs (HRT, TRT, and GLP). Theyâ€™ll pay separate assessment fees and receive consultations from different medical providers.
This doesnâ€™t disrupt our system, but it may cause the â€˜tagâ€™ on Intercom to be inaccurate.
Intercom allows only one tag per patient without issues, and the tag is generated based on the patientâ€™s most recent assessment.
Conversations and messages are routed directly to the care team based on the patient's tag.
Tags can be adjusted as needed for simplicity. For example, a lot of patients register for both HRT and GLP but ultimately proceed with only one.
We can see this by reviewing recent conversations and activity with the patient on Intercom.
We can remove an irrelevant tag on Intercom by simply hovering over the tag on the Intercom sidebar and clicking on 'remove.'
We can add the relevant tag by clicking the + icon under user tags.
Patients Who Have Lab Results
Some new patients or leads may indicate they already have lab results. In the past, we asked patients with lab results to send them to us. However, we frequently encountered issues with the results they sent, leading to the need for them to review the labs with a provider and then have to go in for more labs. This inefficient process ended up wasting visit slots.
If a patient indicates they have labs, please send them the Labs: Can I use my lab results?
Our medical team requests that all patients get a new set of labs. This ensures we have updated values for all the specific tests they'll need to review and often saves time overall. These labs are covered in all states other than New York, New Jersey, Maryland, Rhode Island and Massachusetts.
-If they ask why this is our policy, please send them the macro: Lab Results - Pushback
I totally understand why you'd want to use your recent lab results. That said, we do ask that labs be completed using the order we send, so everything connects smoothly with our system and helps avoid any confusion about which tests were included.
From what weâ€™ve seen, even when patients have had recent bloodwork and our medical team takes a look, some of the key tests needed to safely start treatment are often missingâ€”which usually means the labs need to be redone anyway.
If you have any questions or if thereâ€™s anything we can do to support you, just let us knowâ€”weâ€™re happy to help!
If our messages don't go through or are Undelivered
Message Not Going Through
Sometimes our messages do not get delivered to new sign-ups. If you notice that a message hasn't gone through, please send them an email using the Tech: Message fails to deliver macro:
Hi, this is: from Fountain. Hope you're doing well. We sent a text to you using the number on file: but it appears like our message did not go through. Is: the best phone number to reach you at?
If a provider tries to contact a patient but the message is not going through, please send them an email using the Tech: Outbound texts not going through macro:
Hi, hope you are well. Our medical team tried to text you the link to your video visit at: but the text did not go through. It's possible that you may have a spam filter that is blocking our number. Can you please send a text to: (833) 967-3100 with the message: START please? That may resolve this issue. Thank you!
Undelivered Messages
If we see that a message is not delivered, this means that the patient might have accidentally blocked us. We should send an email to the patient using the following macro: Undelivered Messages: Text Start
Hello - this is Fountain TRT. We sent you a text earlier from 833-967-3100, but it looks like our message did not go through. Is it possible that you blocked our number? If so, please text us at 833-967-3100 with the message: 'START.' Thank you!
If a patient walks into Labcorp, or schedules the appointment themselves, please provide them with their lab order ID.
If patients indicate we've never contacted them/ forgot they signed up
If patients indicate we've never contacted them
Sometimes, new sign-ups register with an incorrect phone number or miss our texts. If they reach out asking why we haven't contacted them, you can send them the Tech: Outbound texts not going through macro:
It looks like we sent a message to the phone number on file: on: Can you confirm that's the best number to reach you at?
If the patient indicates they have a different number, please update their number on Akute. It will sync automatically with Intercom.
Patients Who Forgot They Signed Up
Some patients forget they signed up for our program and are perplexed when we send them a follow-up message asking if they're still interested in getting their labs done.
If they ask, we can send them the General CS: Patients who forget they registered macro:
We're Fountain. You completed our online assessment on: We're reaching out to see if you're still interested in moving forward.
Evaluation Expiration
-There is no 'expiration' for the evaluation. Patients can simply not move forward with the assessment for months/years, and then choose to pick it up again. We would not charge them again for this.
-If a patient went in for labs but never scheduled the visit, they can certainly still do so, regardless of how long it's been. They will need new labwork if it's been more than six months, though, so we'd heavily advise them to get new bloodwork done since it'll be needed to prescribe.

Evaluation Expiration:
Your evaluation does not expire. You can move forward whenever you're ready.
New Labs: Labs Expired
We're glad to hear you'd like to move forward. You're welcome to go ahead with your consultation, but since it's been more than six months since you've done labs, we highly suggest you get new ones. They may be needed for us to prescribe. They will be covered by us (unless you're in NY, NJ, MD.) You can use this link if you'd like to schedule labs: If you'd like to schedule the consultation, regardless, you can - just let us know and we can send you the link. ThankS!
â€‹
NY/NJ/MD patients
Patients in New York, New Jersey, and Maryland will have to use insurance for lab visits at Labcorp. This is made clear to patients in the assessment flow. These states have rules that require patients to do this.
The majority of patients have no issues with this, but some may have questions.
Question/Concern
Macro
Communication
Do you offer at-home blood draws?
NY/NJ Getlabs - Policy
We partner with a mobile Phlebotomy lab, Getlabs. They can perform the bloodwork at your home/office. Your state of residence does not allow client billing for in-person office visits but given that Getlabs performs the bloodwork at your home/office this does not apply.
How much is the cost if I don't have insurance?
NY/NJ: No insurance but wants blood tests
We asked our Labcorp rep what the costs of these tests would be if paid out of pocket last year. The costs were around $250 at the time - but the costs may have since changed. Please be aware that these are the costs for the initial set. Our providers periodically order monitoring labs for patients on treatment to make it's safe and effective. The cost of these tests may be higher.
How frequently are blood tests ordered?
Labs: Blood Draw Frequency
If prescribed, our team reaches out 6 weeks later to schedule labs. Patients then meet with a provider to review the labs and make any needed changes. The next set of labs would be 2-3 months later. Once an effective and safe dose is dialed in, labs are drawn every 5-6 months.
Will my insurance plan cover the cost of the bloodwork? (for TRT)
NY/NJ: Can I use my insurance plan to cover the bloodwork?
We can't comment on your specific insurance plan - but most patients have been able to get the majority of the cost for bloodwork covered through their insurance plan, but may have a small-copay.
The tests ordered are: Testosterone, Testosterone, Free, Direct, Prostate-Specific Ag, Luteinizing Hormone(LH), CBC, Platelet, No Differential, Estradiol.
We'd recommend reaching out to your insurance provider or calling LabCorp at: 800-845-6167.
Will my insurance plan cover the cost of the bloodwork? (for HRT)
HRT: NY/NJ Insurance
We can't comment on your specific insurance plan - but most patients have been able to get the majority of the cost of bloodwork covered through their insurance plan but you may have a small co-pay. The tests ordered are the following: FSH and LH, SHBG, DHEA-S, CBC, Comp. Metabolic Panel, Free and Total Testosterone, Progesterone, Estradiol, TSH, T4, Free T4, T3 We'd recommend reaching out to your insurance provider or calling Labcorp at: 800-845-6167 to see if your insurance will cover the tests.
How can I get them to charge my insurance?
LC - NY/NJ/MD ONLY - How will my insurance be billed?
Labcorp will ask for your insurance card during your visit. They should attempt to bill your insurance for the labwork directly.
Why do I have to pay for the tests? Isn't that included in the evaluation?
NY/NJ: TRT Assessment Fee
The assessment fee covers the cost of labwork (outside of NY, NJ, RI, MA, and MD), consultation, and support. Your state has special rules for in-person lab visits which prevent us from covering the cost of in-person lab work. You'll need to use your insurance for the visit, or go to another state where we can cover the fees.

Lab Orders and Expanded Labs
Lab Tests
The default lab order for TRT patients includes: Testosterone, Testosterone, Free, Direct, Prostate-Specific Ag, Luteinizing Hormone(LH), CBC, Platelet, No Differential, Estradiol
The default lab order for HRT patients includes: FSH, CBC, Platelet, No Differential, Testosterone, Progesterone, Estradiol, TSH
These patients have additional options to pay for expanded coverage, but they have to pay an extra fee.
New signups can order expanded labs. For more info, please refer to this document.
If they decide after registering that they would like to order more tests, we can assist them with this.
We can only order these expanded labs through LabCorp or Getlabs at this time. We do not offer this option to patients in NY, NJ and MD. We also do not provide this option for patients who go to Quest.
Expanded Labs
During registration, patients have the option to select an expanded lab panel. The fees for their labs range from an extra $50 to an extra $375.
If you check under Orders on Akute, you should see that they had the additional lab tests ordered.
The fee for the tests is refundable if they haven't gone in for labs yet. If they have, we cannot refund them (barring exceptional circumstances).

Medical: T Medication Timing
Our providers recommend that patients using T injections should get labs done on any day that works for them other the days they actually inject.
For patients using topical cream, patients should apply the cream at their usual time and have labs drawn at whatever time is most convenient.

My 'T' level is x, do I qualify for treatment?
General CS: Do I qualify for TRT treatment?
I'm not a medical provider so I can't directly answer that. But in general, our providers prescribe based on lots of different variables such as your symptoms, and not just whether T is at a specific number. The reason for this is that every man responds to different testosterone levels differently â€” there's not necessarily one set number every man should be at.

Do you participate with insurance?
Billing: Insurance
Hello - Fountain does not participate with insurance. We certainly understand the reasons to try to find an insurance-based program. However, it doesn't work for Fountain's model for a few reasons. T therapy is frequently not well covered or covered only after a large deductible is paid each year. Treatment decisions are often directed by insurance companies and instead of the shared decisions of patients and providers. Fountain's billing model is based on an all-inclusive fee that recurs periodically and covers all aspects of service (medication, supplies, shipping costs, taxes, provider visits, labs, and 24/7 direct access to providers and support team via our text messages platform). We are able to give more personalized attention and time to fewer individual patients.
If I go to a third-party provider and use my insurance, how much will I need to pay?
General CS: If I go to a third-party provider and use my insurance, how much will I need to pay?
We can't give a specific answer since it would depend on your insurance and the arrangement with your provider.
Do you take HSA/FSA cards?
Billing: FSA/HSA cards
We are happy to provide any information that may help with FSA/HSA reimbursement. Many of our patients have successfully received their fees reimbursed, but we cannot guarantee that FSA/HSA will cover our services. Please let us know if that works for you.

Can coffee affect my results?
Labs: Can coffee affect my results?
Our medical team has indicated the following: The only way coffee can affect your results is by causing dehydration. Specifically, testing of Hemoglobin and Hematocrit can be affected by dehydration. Please be sure to hydrate well with water prior to getting your blood work done.

Pharmacies
Pharmacy Matrix and Internal Communication
Pharmacy Matrix and Internal Communication
Overview
We partner with multiple different pharmacies for different medications.
Some pharmacies use an API system (Application Programming Interface), meaning that our system sends them a signal when to process the order.
The pharmacies that do not use an API system process orders when the RX is sent to them. They all process refills differently - but most are handled through their associated retool pages.
This document contains the list of all active medications we prescribe and their associated pharmacy.
Pharmacy & Prescription Info
Please be aware that the administration route listed is only for internal use. It is not something we should be telling the patient.
If patients have questions regarding their medication that are not specific to a process CS handles, the question should go to the RNs.
Orders Sent To The Wrong Pharmacy
Routine orders should always be sent out to the pharmacies in the Pharmacy and & Prescription Info document.
Occasionally, we submit RXes to local pharmacies, which is not covered here.
If a provider does submit an order to a pharmacy that does not process the medication that the script was sent over (i.e an anastrozole order was over to Empower), they will usually hold the order up and/or contact us.
We must let the ordering provider know they must resubmit the script to the intended pharmacy.
Technical Issues and Internal Communication
Technical Issues and Internal Communication
Most common clarifications with Empower/Belmar can be addressed (i.e., patient's last visit, patient's dob, etc), but if there's anything specific with the script that needs to be amended, it will need to be sent to the provider.
The provider will typically need to resubmit the RX to account for the issue, and the pharmacy should be able to process the order.
If the order is kicked back, we will need to ask the provider to resubmit the RX. If they indicate that they don't know what the issue/unsure how to address it, the ticket can be assigned to Lindsay Burden.
Cross-Communication
The MAs handle refills and clarifications for all pharmacies.
The CS team will still need to contact our partner pharmacies for issues with orders, such as the following:
An order was filled incorrectly
An order was delivered to the wrong address
To update a patient's address with the pharmacy
Anything involving refills or order clarifications needs to go to the MA team that handles the process for that pharmacy.
Please create a refill and clarification ticket for the relevant pharmacy and request.
These tickets will be routed to a few MAs, alongside myself (Daniel Raphael.)
If CS needs to get involved, they may reassign the ticket with a note.
Signature Requirement
The only pharmacy we partner with that has a signature requirement is Curexa. They have a signature requirement for controlled substances (TRT.)
The order can be sent to a FedEx pick-up site if a patient cannot sign.
Some patients may be getting medication from Curexa, but another pharmacy requires them to send the orders to their home address. They will not need to sign for any medication other than TRT, so there shouldn't be any issues.
If a patient specifically requests that their orders be sent out with a signature from another pharmacy, we can email them with that request.
Updating Shipping Addresses
Our partner pharmacies utilize different methods for updating patients' shipping addresses.
Curexa uses an API system that syncs with our system. Once the address has been updated on Akute nothing needs to be done.
Belmar and TPH must be emailed if a patient needs to update their address. If we do not contact them, all orders will continue to be sent to the address on file when the RX was sent over.
Early Refills
The process for early refills differs by pharmacy.
We need to trigger an API call for Curexa if we need to process an early refill for a patient and let them know. Please refer r to the workflow for the specific reason for each (they differ.)
If we need to process an early refill for TPH, Belmar, or Empower, we need to contact the pharmacy and ask them to process an early refill for the patient.
We also need someone to trigger a new order for Belmar prescriptions through their portal.
We will need to provide them with a specific reason for the refill (i.e., dose increase, shipping issue)- otherwise, they will not process it.
We cover the cost of early refills UNLESS it's due to the patient's error (i.e., dropping a bottle and breaking it, throwing out a package accidently.)
Common Pharmacy Issues
Some minor issues often come up with the orders patients receive. These include, but are not limited to:
The order was sent without needles.
The order was sent without alcohol swabs.
In most cases, we must ask the pharmacy to send the remaining supplies.
With Empower Pharmacy or Belmar Pharmacy, it is possible that the RX was sent without needles, so the provider will need to submit an RX for needles specifically.
If the patient would prefer to get needles locally, please refer to the Syringes workflow so we can assist them properly.
Syringes Workflow
They should be able to purchase alcohol swabs at any pharmacy without a prescription.
Order Clarification
Orders that need clarification from our partner pharmacies are kicked back to us. This status may appear on retool (depending on the pharmacy), or the pharmacy's portal.
The pharmacies are supposed to contact us with the clarification required since the status itself is meaningless.
We can contact them preemptively if it's been a few days and we haven't heard back. Once we receive the clarification required, we should assign a ticket to the ordering provider (not the MAs) with the requested clarification.
PO and APO Boxes
Sending medication to PO (Post Office) and APO (Army Post Office) boxes vary based on several factors.
Controlled substances cannot be sent to PO Boxes. We can send non-controlled substances to PO Boxes, but it must go through USPS.
If they are on multiple medications, this would also make the setup impossible since our system can only account for a single delivery address.
If a patient needs the medication shipped to a PO Box, please send them the macro: Orders: Can you deliver to PO boxes?
Our current setup with our pharmacy partners prevents them from sending orders to PO boxes or lock boxes.
Curexa
Can ship to PO and APO boxes.
Empower
Can ship to a PO Box only if it is the patient's physical address. I know some old rural routes still utilize them. In this case, we need a copy of the patient's ID to show it.
Can ship to an APO, as long as the item doesn't require cold-shipping.
The Pharmacy Hub
Can ship to PO Boxes as long as they're not iced or cold medications/medications that require ice packs to ship.
Can't ship to APO/military PO Boxes.
